#+TITLE: Buck accounting correction: unified implementation plan
#+DATE: 2026-11-08
#+OPTIONS: ^:nil toc:3

* Start here: what remains before the sandboxes and the five-year runs

Written 2026-11-12 for a session that knows nothing of the work so far. Read
this section, then the tail of this file (the latest milestone, its checkpoint
and its observations). The sections between are the history and the design
record; consult them, do not re-derive them.

** Where things stand

Worktree =.claude/worktrees/buck-compound=, branch
=feature/household-minimal-ecosystem=, nothing pushed. Canonical =src/Buck.sol=
(unsigned compounded cash, per-NFT issuance, external Jubilee) is the
foundation. One Solidity change has been made since it was qualified:
=burn(0)= releases coverage (the release milestone).

The household is built from /components/ on one owner dispatcher and one
receipt cursor (=HouseholdRefinance=). Where each stands:

| Debtor refinancing, cash-only resumption | refinance | qualified | qualified |
| Surplus: save, top up, redeem, discounted retirement | surplus | qualified | qualified |
| Working balance (wallet) | wallet | qualified | qualified |
| =burn(0)= release, SDK =release= | (SDK) | qualified | qualified |

The normal decision loop is *open*: =CreditHouseholdAgent.act= runs the
dispatcher on canonical accounting in both languages, and the first complete
30-day Python world has run (the opened-loop milestone at the end). *No
five-year run exists yet on the corrected accounting*; step C lengthens the
short world, step D runs the campaigns.

** The economy we are modelling: participants and the natural K (owner, 2026-11-14)

The owner's view, after the one-year worlds' falling K:

- /H1. The natural K is unknown./ A falling K may be the economy seeking its
  equilibrium; no one knows yet where K rests in a varied BUCK economy. Run
  from K0 = 0.5 as well as 0.75.
- /H2. The basket is the BUCK's T-bill./ Its rebalancing and arbitrage
  returns are the BUCK economy's risk-free return. An owner with a better
  opportunity does what the basket does: buys assets, insures them, issues
  BUCK against them, buys more; when it divests it must buy BUCK to retire
  its liens, which is BUCK demand.
- /H3. The worlds are bootstrap-sized./ One mortgage converted BUCK -> USDC
  is a large share of the whole economy; in a mature one most flows are
  BUCK <-> assets (TOKEN), and the basket's large standing position answers
  K: a falling K should make the basket pull BUCK out of circulation.
- /H4. Behaviour by signal./ BVIB > 1 (BUCK cheap): sell assets for BUCK;
  do not retire liens early (their relief is a risk-free 2% a year), do not
  sit on large balances (2% a year demurrage), deposit in the basket. BVIB <
  1 (BUCK dear): sell BUCK, buy assets, issue more credit and buy more,
  until equilibrium.
- /H5. A third of the participants is missing./ Wage earners paying down
  mortgages and saving (the households: have), enterprises issuing BUCK
  credit to fund their businesses (missing), and holders of portfolios
  drawing them down for retirement or keeping them for heirs (the investor
  covers only accumulation). With a realistic mix K should settle where the
  flows balance.

What the contract and the runs say:

- /F1. The rates are as stated./ =NetDemurrageMath=: balances decay by
  exp(-0.02 / year); relief accrues linearly over 50 years, 2% of principal a
  year. A lien is cheaper than free; idle BUCK costs 2% a year.
- /F2. K does not bound the basket's held BUCK, and that is nearly all of its
  lien./ The basket's limit is its contract's =headroom()=: K times its
  credit's mark, less its lien. Under the net-mark policy the mark is its
  equity (LOW marks, less unpaid relief) plus the BUCK it holds in its own
  pool positions over K (=BuckBasketEquityStorage._creditValueS=: "Buck
  allows a lien of K x the mark, so adding the held BUCKs over K makes that K
  x equity + held"). So the limit is K x equity + held BUCK, and K bounds
  only the lien the basket no longer holds. Measured exactly on 2026-11-14:
  mark 14.13M at K 0.752 and 21.12M at K 0.402, against equity + held / K of
  14.15M and 21.2M. In every world the basket holds more BUCK than it owes:
  (lien - held) / equity ended at -0.076 (reverting year), -0.059 (history),
  -0.058 (K0 = 0.5, day 230, K 0.35), while K binds only when that ratio
  reaches K. So K never restrained the basket, the source of 70% of new
  supply (observation 45), at any K reached; it bound only the debtors, from
  day 150 (observation 40). The controller cuts a limit the largest issuer is
  exempt from: this explains the monotonic fall better than tolerance or
  inflation (observations 42 and 44). And H3's response could not happen even
  at the limit: the wheel trims back under it only inside its
  liquidity-shortfall branch (=BuckBasketEquityWheel.sol=). The BUCK held in
  the TOKEN/BUCK pools is what a TOKEN seller receives and what prices BVIB,
  so it is in circulation for the market even though the policy treats it as
  the basket's own (owner's question, recommendation 68).
- /F3. Every organic flow crosses the dollar route./ Debtors sell BUCK for
  USDC (their mortgages and wages are in dollars), investors buy BUCK with
  USDC, the basket buys TOKEN with BUCK. No agent buys TOKEN as an input or
  sells output for BUCK, so the BUCK <-> asset circulation of H3 is absent
  and a $10M BUCK/USDC pool carries the economy's organic flow.
- /F4. H4's cheap-BUCK deposit adds supply today./ With the basket
  redeploying deposits at L/E 0.82, a BUCK deposited while BUCK is cheap
  returns 0.82 BUCK of new issuance to the TOKEN/BUCK pools (observation 43);
  the deposit is the right private choice and a weak public one until K
  binds the basket.

What to build and test, in this order:

1. /The natural K (H1)./ The same one-year centre from K0 = 0.5 on both price
   paths, beside the K0 = 0.75 years: one K reached from above and below is
   the natural K of this mix; both falling means the drift is structural
   (F2). Run on 2026-11-14 (=build/runs/economy-365-k050{,-revert}=,
   observation 60): from 0.5, K fell all year too, to 0.249 (history) and
   0.276 (reverting), against 0.445 and 0.469 from 0.75. The four years do
   not converge (0.25 apart at the start, about 0.2 at the end) and none
   flattens: no natural K appears while K cannot reach the basket (F2).
2. /Decide whether K bounds the basket's held BUCK (F2, recommendation 68)./
   The owner's expectation (H3) is a basket that answers K: a falling K makes
   it pull BUCK out of circulation (withdraw the BUCK side of its positions,
   sell TOKEN for BUCK, retire lien). Under the net mark it never has to. The
   choices to try live, each for a year from both K0: count held BUCK against
   K (mark the credit at equity alone); or keep the net mark and have the
   wheel deleverage whenever headroom is negative, not only when liquidity is
   short; or bound held BUCK by its own share of K.
3. /The enterprise (H2, H5)./ A third agent role: an owner with a project
   return r (a keyed draw, say normal about 10% a year) compares it with the
   basket's trailing return and the cost of BUCK credit (negative: relief
   less the premium deposit's forgone return); while r is higher it insures
   the asset under construction, draws its K-scaled credit and buys its
   inputs as TOKEN (LABR, CNST, NRGC are labour, construction and energy),
   faster when the BUCK is dear (BVIB < 1, inputs cheap in BUCK), slower when
   cheap; on completion it sells the output, buys BUCK and retires the lien.
   Its flows are BUCK <-> TOKEN, both ways, timed by BVIB; consumed inputs
   leave the pools (a sink), the feed and whale restoring TOKEN/USDC.
4. /The portfolio holder (H5)./ Generalise the investor: a large opening
   portfolio (basket shares, BUCK, dollars), an income drawn from it (about
   4% a year), rebalancing between the basket and dollar assets on relative
   value (BVIB, the basket's return), a bequest motive setting the drawdown.
5. /A mature opening (H3)./ Besides today's transition economy (dollar
   mortgages refinanced into BUCK), a world that opens already circulating:
   households with BUCK liens and basket shares, wages partly in BUCK,
   enterprises mid-project, pools deep enough that one household is under
   0.1% of a day's flow. Compare where K settles in each.
6. /The population mix as cells./ Wage earners, enterprises and portfolio
   holders in thirds at the centre, and the corners, each from both K0.

*The basket against the owner's vision (2026-11-14).* The owner: the basket
issues BUCK against its equity and deploys them (rebalancing, arbitrage) for
return; a later fall in K makes no call on it. When a holder retires a
position and the basket cannot issue BUCK to pay it, the exit is paid in
kind, its share of the holdings, so no remaining holder is made worse off. As
K falls, rebalancing and arbitrage go on, but BUCK flowing in produce fewer
new BUCK because the credit is under water by some measure. The Jubilee fund
keeps little cash (a target of about 1%), pays large reliefs in chunks, and
recharges from demurrage and by selling its basket position. How the code
stands (=src/basket/BuckBasketEquity*.sol=, =BasketUpkeepFacet.sol=,
=src/wheel/JubileeKind.sol=, =src/JubileeFund.sol=):

| The vision | Today | Match |
|------------+-------+-------|
| Issue against equity, deploy for return | The upkeep pairs every TOKEN with issued BUCK at once (the BUCK stay in the position), sizes the book against parity, and the wheel places spare liquidity (=_fundCash= issues up to the room =_issuableS=) | yes |
| No call when K falls | Nothing forces repayment; a K below the lien only stops new room. The trim refills liquidity "first back under the limit" when liquidity is short, a self-correction, not a call | yes |
| BUCK exits paid from what the basket can pay, else in kind, no burden on others | Exits never issue: paid from wallet cash if it suffices (=_exit=), else pro rata in kind (=_exitInKind=: a fraction of every position and idle TOKEN; the leaver leaves TOKEN covering its share of the lien; released BUCK stay as cash). Cash is refilled between exits by issuance while K allows | yes, but |
| ... and an under-water basket still lets holders leave | =_exitSnapshot= reverts every exit, cash or in kind, when the credit is short before or after (=CreditShortfallIncreased=). The net mark keeps that from happening in practice (it has not fired in any run so far) | no |
| As K falls, inflows issue fewer BUCK: under water "by some measure" | The measure is the net mark's: K bounds the lien less the BUCK held in the basket's own positions (=_creditValueS=). The upkeep leans against parity: while the BUCK is cheap it aims to hold BUCK, and it did, the lien less held BUCK at -5% to -8% of equity all year. So inflows add no net issuance; the gross issuance (about 0.8 BUCK per BUCK deposited) is pool depth beside TOKEN, unrestrained by K | in net terms |
| The basket retires lien from cash it does not need | =JubileeKind='s retirement job (=retireCredit= through the fund's =reduce=, chunked across blocks) exists; the worlds deploy it disabled (=retirementCap= 0), so the lien never shrinks and its relief only accrues (observation 56) | built, off |
| Jubilee keeps little cash, chunks large reliefs, recharges | Reserve target 10% of the accrued claim (=reserveTargetBp= 1000); excess invested in its basket receipt (budget half-life a day, 1% threshold); each relief at most 10% of its cash (=ReliefLiquidityLimit=; the tab's "relief available"), plus a shared per-block budget, so large ones chunk; recharged by demurrage and by the wheel's scheduled sale of receipt shares (at most 10% of the receipt a job) | shape yes; 10% not 1% |

Corrections to earlier readings: F2 stands as arithmetic (K never binds the
basket's gross lien) but the net mark is deliberate and its lean already
holds the basket net long BUCK while the BUCK is cheap, so the basket is not
simply "issuing into a cheap BUCK"; what keeps BVIB above 1 needs a flow
decomposition by actor (debtors' conversions, investors' purchases,
arbitrage, the wheel), recommendation 69. Observation 56's unpaid relief is
not the fund failing to pay: relief is paid only when principal is retired
or renewed, and the basket never retires (its job is off); the 6k "available"
is the 10%-of-cash cap per operation.

Gaps against the vision, smallest first: (1) turn the basket's retirement job
on (configuration only: =deploy.basket_retirement_cap_m=, added
2026-11-14; a one-year world with it on is running,
=build/runs/economy-365-retire=); (2) let an under-water basket's holders
leave in kind instead of refusing them (a contract change in =_exitSnapshot=
and the post-exit check: "no greater shortfall" rather than "no shortfall");
(3) decide whether K should reach the gross lien, which needs (2) first
(otherwise every exit is refused) and costs the basket pool depth and fee
income; (4) the Jubilee reserve target, 10% today against the owner's ~1%.

*The owner's decisions on exits, relief and the reserve (2026-11-14).*

- /Exits always proceed, and default to BUCK./ A holder retiring a position
  is paid in BUCK: from the basket's cash when it suffices; otherwise its
  fraction of every position is withdrawn and its TOKEN sold for BUCK, the
  holder bearing the slippage. The sale moves the TOKEN/BUCK pools away from
  the USDC routes; the wheel's arbitrage (and the basket's own LP positions,
  which absorb the sale) harvest that for the holders who stay. A holder may
  ask for its share in kind instead; BUCK is the default because the users
  of the basket (the Jubilee fund first) need BUCK, not LABR.
- /The one gate is the lien./ The TOKEN back the basket's lien (BUCK's rule:
  an operation may not worsen a facility's K shortfall). So an exit retires
  as much of the basket's principal as keeps the shortfall from worsening,
  from the BUCK its own share releases and raises (none while the basket has
  room under K, today's behaviour; when under water, K times the collateral
  leaving). The only exit that reverts is one whose assets, at executable
  prices, cannot cover that, or whose slippage passes the holder's own
  minimum. The holders who stay keep their equity per share and their
  shortfall does not grow.
- /Unpaid relief is equity, not collateral./ It counts in the basket's
  equity and share price (it already does: =buckEq= = cash - lien + relief).
  It becomes credit only when crystallised: an owner closes its BuckCredit
  (receiving the relief) and opens a new one on the larger equity. For the
  basket, crystallising is retiring principal (its retirement job), after
  which the smaller lien leaves more room. So =_creditValueS= keeps excluding
  unpaid relief, as it does today.
- /The Jubilee reserve stays at 10%/ of the accrued claim, each relief at most
  10% of its cash; 90% deployed in the basket is the intended use.

Design for implementation (the contract work, then both SDKs and the
households' exit staging):

1. =BuckBasketEquity= has 406 bytes left under the code limit, so the exit's
   conversion moves into a facet (the venue facet has =monetaryLeg=, a direct
   TOKEN -> BUCK swap per constituent, and 2.5 kB free).
2. Order of payment: cash; else withdraw the fraction of every position (pro
   rata: it spreads the sale over all pools, which minimises the holder's
   slippage, and leaves the remaining mix unchanged; an over-weight tilt
   would rebalance but cost the holder more) and sell its TOKEN through each
   constituent's own pool. The direct route is the one that creates the
   displacement the wheel's arbitrage harvests; routing through USDC can
   follow if measured slippage warrants it.
3. Retire principal at exit only as needed to hold the shortfall (=burn= of
   the released BUCK against the basket's facility, with BUCK's own
   relief settlement); replace =_exitSnapshot='s "no shortfall before or
   after" with "no greater shortfall, and no lower equity per remaining
   share at TWAP marks".
4. An in-kind request (a separate entry point, keeping =redeem= /
   =redeemShares= BUCK-only) delivers TOKEN for the holder's net equity, its
   lien share settled in BUCK as above.
5. Tests first: an under-water exit proceeds in BUCK; the shortfall does not
   grow; the remaining holders' equity per share does not fall; the leaver
   bears slippage and its =minBuck= binds; the in-kind request; unpaid
   relief in equity and not in collateral; the exit gate in the households' staging (it no longer waits
   on a closed door). A contract change: the full qualification (=make
   nix-test=, contracts-dist, both SDKs, the Tevm files) and a release.

Running many worlds on datasim.local: every cell writes its run record as
it goes, so a dashboard on that machine reads them all while they grow
(=--runs= at the campaign's =records= directory). Its live control is
loopback-only, so reach it through an SSH tunnel (=ssh -L
8890:127.0.0.1:8890 datasim.local=), which keeps it loopback on both ends.
Two additions make that the normal path: the dashboard listing several
runs directories (or a campaign tree) grouped by campaign; and a control
channel in the run directory itself (=control.jsonl=, read by the CLI loop
between days), so batch cells can be steered like dashboard sessions.

** How to work here

- /Two languages, one behaviour./ Every change is mirrored:
  =alberta_buck/sim/net_household.py= and =core/js/src/agents/net-household.js=
  (assessors and the receipt machine); =credit_households.py= and
  =credit-household.js= (the agent and dispatchers); =household_policy.py= and
  =household-policy.js= (pure policy); =household_calendar.py= and
  =household-calendar.js=; =core/python/buck_core/= and =core/js/src/= (the SDK;
  it never imports =alberta_buck=). Shared vectors live in
  =test/fixtures/household-*.json= and are read by both test suites. Decide
  amounts in integers; where a float must cross the language boundary,
  quantize first (the wallet takes its discount in parts per billion).
- /Phases./ Finish one component in both languages, qualify it, write what it
  taught about the five-year runs and the sandboxes into this file (measured
  facts with their fixture, then recommendations), /then/ commit. ASCII commit
  messages, no attribution lines, no push; merges to master are =--no-ff=.
- /Qualify with whole targets./ A focused subset once hid a failing test for
  five checkpoints.

  | Command | What | Time |
  |---------+------+------|
  | =make nix-venv-test-python PYTEST_FILES="<the twenty files below>" PYTEST_ARGS='--basetemp=build/test/NAME -q'= | Supported Python set | 2 min |
  | =make nix-core-equilibrium-policy-test= | Fast JavaScript policy, quote, calendar and dashboard units (83) | 1 min |
  | =make nix-core-equilibrium-bootstrap-test= | Actual JavaScript, eight Tevm fixture files together (132 tests) | 50 min |
  | =... CORE_EQUILIBRIUM_TEST_FILES=test/net-household-wallet.tevm.test.js= | One file (exit 2 min, wallet 4, resume 11, programme 21, surplus 19, invest 40, bootstrap 36) | |
  | =make nix-venv-net-demurrage-smoke= | The repayment engine, Python and JavaScript | 5 min |
  | =make nix-core-demo-eqworld= | Browser bundle rebuilds | 1 min |
  | =make nix-test= | All Solidity (needed again once a contract changes) | long |

  The twenty Python files: =core/python/tests/= =test_net_market_quote=,
  =test_artifacts=, =test_router=, =test_session=, =test_net_purchase_outcome=;
  =alberta_buck/test/= =test_household_policy=, =test_household_calendar=,
  =test_net_basket_mark=, =test_net_household_setup=,
  =test_net_household_invest=, =test_net_household_exit=,
  =test_net_economy_report=, =test_sim_report=, =test_settlement=,
  =test_net_repayment_target=, =test_net_demurrage_smoke=, =test_net_market=,
  =test_net_economy_bootstrap=, =test_runrecord=, =test_dashboard_live=
  (313 pass at the live-control checkpoint, none expected to fail. =test_economy.py= is not in
  the set: its short-world test still expects legacy accounting.)
- /Traps that cost time./
  - pyrevm refuses any =eth_call= whose sender is a contract, and every
    simulated household is a proxy. Simulate "as the owner" through
    =d.net_simulate= / =world.netMarket.simulate=. Tevm accepts such a call.
  - A household's fair value is the basket's BUCK price times the /same/
    dollar pool's spot. A BUCK/USDC-only trade cancels out and is never a
    discount or a premium. To move the signal, trade the same share of every
    BUCK pool and let the basket's TWAP pass.
  - The Python =household_world= fixture has 100k-BUCK TOKEN pools and no
    basket lien; the JavaScript builder bootstraps a leveraged basket with
    1M pools. Size test trades to the seeding helper's 239k BUCK.
  - A calendar refuses to skip a payday. Fixtures pin =pay_day= (see below);
    step tests onto each payday, never over one.
  - Tevm's clock moves with every transaction, pyrevm's only on a warp. In
    JavaScript, compare a balance read before a payday with a small tolerance.
  - A qualification run reads Python and JavaScript sources for its whole
    duration. Edit only documents until it reports.
  - Recording history must never interrupt money: record after the books and
    pending evidence are saved, and make the recording non-fatal.
  - The legacy JavaScript household tests (=credit-household.tevm.test.js=,
    =eqsim.tevm.test.js=) already fail on this branch with =no artifact for
    Permit2=: their packaged artifact loader predates the canonical world.
    They are not in any target above. Migrate or retire them (step B's
    remainder); do not mistake them for regressions.
- /Diagnosing./ Write a scratch pytest that imports =household_world= and the
  helpers from =alberta_buck/test/test_net_household_setup.py= and prints what
  you need (end it with =assert 0= and pass =-s=); run it with the same Make
  target, its path in =PYTEST_FILES=. Printing each record of
  =agent.refinance_machine.events= (=seq=, =kind=, =phase=, =status=,
  =operation=, =reason=, =amounts=) shows a whole scenario.
- /Reading a world./ =python -m alberta_buck.sim.digest <vector.json>...= (in
  the venv) prints K, BVIB and price over the run, the supply change split
  into the basket's lien, household principal and other, who holds the BUCK
  at the start and the end (BUCK/USDC pool, TOKEN/BUCK pools, basket,
  everyone else), each role's
  holdings, actions and net worth against its counterfactual, and the kinds
  of refusal recorded. No EVM; reads only recorded frames.
- /Watching a run./ =make nix-venv-dashboard= (=http://127.0.0.1:8890/=)
  shows every run record under =build/runs=, finished or growing; every
  =python -m alberta_buck.sim= run writes one, and =make nix-venv-run-import
  VECTORS=...= makes one from an earlier vector. See =README-simulations.org=,
  "Run records and the dashboard". Never bind the tunnel's ports (8000,
  8797, 8798); the server refuses them.
- /Long runs and editing./ The six-file JavaScript acceptance takes 30 to 40
  minutes and reads only JavaScript, fixtures and artifacts. Start it alone
  and work on Python meanwhile; never edit JavaScript, a fixture it reads or
  rebuild contracts until it reports. A new fixture file is safe. A
  one-year Python world (about 45 minutes) can run beside it.
- /After a contract change./ =make nix-build=, the focused
  =make nix-match-<Contract>Test=, =make nix-test=,
  =make nix-contracts-dist-check=, =make nix-sim-build=, then every target in
  the table: the simulations deploy the rebuilt artifacts.

** Remaining work, in order

Steps 0, 1, A and B are done; C has run its one-year worlds and waits on the
owner's answer to recommendation 53. The owner's direction of 2026-11-14 put
step F (one run record, one dashboard, fewer tools) ahead of the campaigns:
R1 (the run record and the Overview) and R2 (live control) are done; R3 (the
component tabs) comes next, and these are how the campaigns will be watched
and steered. Each step is a
phase: implement in both languages, qualify, write the observations here,
then commit.

*** 0. Paydays (done)

Each household is paid on its own keyed day of the 30-day month (=pay_day=,
=first_payday= / =firstPayday=). See the payday milestone at the end.

*** 1. =burn(0)= releases what it can (done; contract change)

=burn(0)= and =burn(0, ids)= release every selected facility's coverage that
no principal needs at the freshly computed K and refund the premium deposit
in the same proportion. =src/lib/BuckRenewalOps.sol= holds =planRelease= and
=release= (=BuckFacilities= has 633 bytes left under the code limit);
=quoteBurn(0, ids)= returns the plan; event =FacilityReleased=. SDK, both
languages: =quote_release= / =release= / =release_from_receipt= (JavaScript
=quoteRelease= / =release= / =releaseFromReceipt=) and =ReleasePending=. The
asset exit is its only caller. See the release milestone.

*** A. Mirror credit investment, exits and the investor in JavaScript (done)

Ported function for function (see the opened-loop milestone). JavaScript
tests: =core/js/test/net-household-invest.tevm.test.js= and
=net-household-exit.tevm.test.js=, now in =CORE_EQUILIBRIUM_TEST_FILES=, on the
shared fixtures; cases marked ="js": false= run in Python only, to bound the
Tevm run. The JavaScript world bootstraps 1M-deep TOKEN pools with a
leveraged basket: thresholds in the premium and investor fixtures were set
from the Python world and are asserted, not assumed, in JavaScript.

*** B. Open the act loop (done)

=act= runs =_act_net= / =actNet= on canonical accounting in both languages.
Recovery comes first (a recovered or uncertain outcome consumes the day),
then the calendar, then the components in order (see the opened-loop
milestone and =README-households.org=, "The daily dispatcher"). Still to do
here: migrate or retire the legacy JavaScript household tests
(=credit-household.tevm.test.js=, =eqsim.tevm.test.js=; they fail on a missing
=Permit2= artifact and are in no target), and run a JavaScript population
world once (=make nix-core-eqsim=) to measure its cost per household-day.

*** C. Short complete worlds (one-year worlds run; gate open)

=make nix-venv-economy-smoke ECONOMY_SMOKE_DAYS=30= runs the working tree and
writes =build/sim/economy-smoke.{json,pdf}=; lengthen it by stages (90, 365,
730 days). The first 30-day world ran on 2026-11-13 (observations 29 to 35):
two failure kinds were found and fixed (a day's household mark crashing on a
refused relief quote; deposit minimums priced from the wrong share price).
90-day and 365-day worlds then completed (observations 36 to 41;
=ECONOMY_SMOKE_OUT=build/sim/economy-smoke-365.json=, about 45 minutes on
pyrevm). Variants of the year, each one =--set key=value= on the smoke's
command (=python -m alberta_buck.sim --experiment ...economy.toml --backend
pyrevm --days 365 --set ... --out ...=, in the venv): every tolerance 0
(observation 42), the reverting price path (observation 44). *Gate before step D:* K falls 0.75 ->
0.45 to 0.55 over the year in all three, BVIB sits 1 to 3% above 1, and the
basket issues about 0.7 BUCK per BUCK deposited, on cheap days too, into the
TOKEN/BUCK pools (observations 43 and 45). Recommendation 53 says what to
find first and the owner's choices. Done: supply equals open plus deferred
principal exactly in all three, and no household's event log holds a
dispatcher fault (=kind= =act=); the fault counter itself reached the frames
only afterwards (observation 46). Still to check: every household's books reconcile (=unwound= equals the sum of
confirmed reductions; wallet, receipts and principal against chain); the
report charts the new records (investment bites, exits, releases);
then the measurements the recommendations ask for. Add the broad sell-off and
the broad purchase (the same share of every BUCK pool) to the shock set.
=ShockAgent= still uses the numeric =mint= overload and its own swap callback:
verify it on canonical money or replace it with an intervention (step F).

*** D. The five-year campaigns

Commit first: campaigns freeze their sources. Then, per
=README-simulations.org=: =make nix-venv-economy-plan CELLS=centre=,
=make nix-venv-economy-run CAMPAIGN=baseline CELLS=centre JOBS=2=, the grid,
the policies and the stress cells, and =make nix-venv-economy-report=. Run
=make nix-venv-economy-accounting-test=, =make nix-venv-net-demurrage-smoke=
and =make nix-test= at the exact revision and record the counts with the
campaign. Python on pyrevm is the five-year vehicle; a JavaScript population
world is far slower (one debtor's refinance, save and prepayment took 300 s
under Tevm).

*** E. The savings sandbox and the JavaScript equilibrium demo

The savings sandbox is the Python economy behind a page:
=make nix-venv-sim-savings SIM_SAVINGS_PORT=<an unused port>=, acceptance
=make nix-venv-sandbox-savings-test SAVINGS_TEST_PORT=<an unused port>=
(=README-sandboxes.org=). It needs step B and nothing else structural, then:
its stress buttons lean on BUCK/USDC only, so households respond after
arbitrage carries the move into the basket's price, and the page should show
the dollar price beside the cost/fair ratio; a lost reply stops a household,
wages included, so the page needs that status and a reconcile action. Never
start, stop or probe the tunnel origin ports 8000, 8797 or 8798. The
JavaScript demo (=make nix-core-demo-eqworld-serve=, =make nix-core-eqsim=)
needs step B in JavaScript before a population is practical.

Duplication: two timeline engines (=loop.run=, =driver.SimDriver=); four grid

*The owner's direction of 2026-11-14:* one run record and one dashboard for
every test world, simulation and sandbox, readable after a run or while it
grows, with a tab per ecosystem component (the Jubilee fund, the BuckBasket,
the insurers and their pools, the agents in aggregate) and a drill-down to one
agent's state and its state-machine steps; ideally interactive (add
participants, change their settings, apply shocks). Live engine: the Python
session server, local first. The same page must also run on a small Tevm world
in the browser tab with fewer capabilities: an in-browser world is the only way
many people can each run a BUCK economy without loading a central server, so
that path is kept. Use the work to bring the tools into one format, location
and interface, retiring duplicates.

* Direction, source and current state

The user's 2026-11-04 decision is to retain =src/Buck.sol= as the foundation,
porting the correct demurrage, per-NFT issuance and Jubilee discoveries from
BuckNet into it while preserving Buck's useful functionality. This
*supersedes* the earlier plan to complete the BuckNet facade and then replace
Buck with it. The existing filename is retained so all migration references
continue to point to one authoritative checklist. The earlier plan and API
simplification proposals remain available at =32c7d17a= in Git; the progress
and verification log below is preserved.

Continue in =feature/household-minimal-ecosystem=, branched from the clean
calendar-qualified =6bf53283= on =feature/buck-compound-demurrage=, at
=.claude/worktrees/buck-compound=, created from the reviewed foundation plan
=5096c7b4=. The foundation and BuckNet worktrees and unrelated main-checkout
changes remain intact. Buck now uses unsigned compounded cash and actual
per-NFT issuance with the external Jubilee interface. Both original numeric
mint/burn overloads, both quotes, approvals/identity receipts, sponsorship and
the controller reserve remain. The focused canonical-token regressions and
Carrying/sponsored burn arithmetic run against Buck itself. Existing fund,
renewal, maturity and cash-basket checks now run against Buck, and shared
Python/JS deployment and the browser wallet are restored. Both basket upkeep
facets and the separate desk now use explicit cash/principal transitions;
real V3 and actual Jubilee settlement checks cover their contract paths.
Fund/maturity work and optional actual basket retirement are now scheduled in
both existing wheels. Public basket onboarding now validates the canonical
model, verified public identity and matching BuckCredit hook, with shared
setup in the existing upkeep facets and no deployed initializer. Production
registry authorization and both public V3 lifecycles pass. The canonical
machine basket is now an approved Carrying issuer. Its nominal ERC-20 flow
uses one shared helper for net valuation/budgets/payouts and funded net-ROI
arb, covering both directions of triangles and four-pool TOKEN routes. Both
primary runtimes enable arb by default and use bounded transactions on the
same wheel; detailed invariants and final acceptance are recorded below. The canonical
machine wheel now has production identity authorization/revocation and real
private-keeper reward tests. Canonical uint256 cash/baseline math now agrees
across Solidity, Rust, Python and Node/browser WASM. Both existing machine
builders now reuse that monetary graph for deployment and bootstrap. Python
also configures the separate desk and retains its component comparisons.
A short existing Python economy now reports canonical cash/principal and
exact open receipt marks through completed JSON/PDF generation. Complete
household lifecycles and full economy restoration remain open. All three
household roles now initialize actual ordinary accounts and distinct facilities
in both languages. Their shared SDK credit mark separates principal, cash,
guarded relief and each facility's own insurer-pool refund value; normal
decisions still fail explicitly before income until issuance/exits migrate.
Both languages now report complete personal financial portfolios: all ordinary
custody accounts, actual receipt basis/marks, premiums, claims and liabilities,
with exact integer USD conversion. Python captures these books daily; both
interactive and batch drivers refuse unported decisions before completion.
Their primary proxy markets now reuse guarded net buys, bounded user-paid
sales and separate authenticated USDC payment helpers with real Permit2.
JS reuses selected-NFT repayment/recovery. Both households now execute an
explicit assessed refinance with optional guarded reserve acquisition,
rechecked selected-NFT issuance, bounded export and exact USDC bank payment.
Confirmed phases apply once with exact public histories; missing evidence
stops retries. Canonical external insurance costs track only the activated
share, while the mortgage counterfactual keeps its full premium. Economic
assessment now chooses actual bounded candidates with explicit forecasts,
including wallet/new/mixed funding, proportional insurance and reserve
costs. Default forecasts now observe the actual global fund/age ratio with a
labeled idle-cash carry baseline; full overrides remain explicit comparisons.
The explicit calendar component now schedules arrival capital and recurring
wages/bills/mortgage transfers with receipt recovery and independent shadow
books. Dated bank evidence corrects interest from its effective payment day.
Both Python runners and the JS day clock now publish one household epoch
and use absolute targets, preventing wheel waits or fractional ticks from
accumulating calendar drift. Later arrivals share that same date anchor.
The explicit automatic debtor component now reuses that dispatcher/calendar,
retaining keyed adoption timing and seeking fresh bounded tranches daily
after issuance. Reserve-safe saving, basket redemption and discounted
retirement now run on that same receipt cursor in both languages, with the
selected-facility engine's reductions in the public history and the lasting
credit front kept apart from each operation's own protection. The BUCK
working balance is tended after each payday in its own custody on that same
cursor. Saver credit investment and asset exits still gate the normal
household loop. Full restoration is deferred, not a prerequisite for uninterrupted
interactive and five-year acceptance (November 8 priority below).
Nominal issuance references and net liquidation/buyback units have distinct
real-pool vectors; no controller-policy change is required by that distinction.
Complete household lifecycle,
five-year evidence and Notes/release checks remain separate gates below.

The browser-only wallet sandbox now uses canonical Buck, with separate cash
and liability displays and verified format-4 restoration. The five-year
economy, Python savings sandbox and JavaScript leveraged equilibrium demo
still have legacy household/persistence assumptions. Personal JS records are
migrated; the complete action lifecycle and restoration remain open. Their deployment
and bootstrap now use Buck's explicit Jubilee dependency and linked libraries. No five-year campaign
has validated the corrected accounting, and the
canonical cash-only diagnostic is not the leveraged household economy. Changing
the implementation behind the name Buck will not make old saved results or

** Comparison used to choose the foundation

| Source | Inspected revision | Relevant finding |
|--------+--------------------+------------------|
| Master =src/Buck.sol= | =9351961d=; source blob =d2b95debf8c2= | Original identity/ERC-20, allocation, funding and sponsorship foundation. Token relief comes from BuckCredit, but the NFT ages activated coverage, not actual BUCK principal. Account demurrage is linear on gross balances; signed credit drawing already exists despite the unsigned wording of some comments. |
| =feature/net-demurrage-model:src/Buck.sol= | =291560c1=; source blob =22ab4b169967= | Contains the later spendable-amount mint/burn interface, premium-based reserve gate, exhausted-balance fixes and explicit simulation-hook separation. Relief was moved to the holder's signed lien, which must now be replaced. |
| Buck in =feature/bucknet-economy= | =32c7d17a=; same blob =22ab4b169967= | Byte-for-byte the model-branch Buck, so its functionality has not been overwritten. This is the working source foundation. |
| BuckNet and related code | =32c7d17a= | Donors for net decay, Carrying values, per-NFT issuance/age, marked cash-limited Jubilee relief, insurer pools, renewal, maturity and regression tests. The facade still lacks complete Buck functionality. |

Use the current Buck source and compare against master for preservation; do
not reset the tree to master or copy its obsolete accounting wholesale.
Master's separation of token money and NFT relief is the useful structural
precedent. Its relief *base* still needs correction from activated insured
coverage to actually issued BUCK, including any newly issued premium funding.

The model branch removes master's =setBasket=/=mintFromBasket=/=burnFromBasket=
from production Buck into =src/legacy/BuckWithBasketHooks.sol=. It adds
holder-level =reliefOf=/=redeemCost=/=settleRelief= and =totalIssued=. Retaining
Buck does not require reintroducing unbacked production issuance or preserving
the subsequently rejected holder-relief mechanism.

* Accounting to transplant, and what can disappear

Let =Q= be =1e36= fractional units per six-decimal raw BUCK, =Y= be 365.25 days,
=T= be 50 years, =S= be outstanding issued principal, and =D(dt)= be the tested
=NetDemurrageMath.decay= factor: continuously applied nominal 2% per year.
Do not change to a different annual-compounding convention during this port.

** Wallets: replace exposure history with unsigned net value

An ordinary unsponsored account needs one current net balance and a checkpoint,
not a signed balance or the integral of its holding history:

#+begin_example
N(now) = decay(N(checkpoint), elapsed)
balanceOf(ordinary) = floor(N(now) / Q)
#+end_example

An incoming BUCK is cash even if its recipient owes principal on an NFT.
Transfers never issue credit or automatically repay that NFT. Mint explicitly
issues BUCK, including unspent BUCK, and burn explicitly extinguishes it.
Unissued capacity is a distinct credit view and is excluded from balanceOf.
A K change affects capacity and settlement eligibility, not existing cash.

The BuckNet ledger's separate received =held= and issued =unspent= components
can be combined for token accounting: the financial checks in the quoter,
operations and swap guard use their sum. NFTs already retain all debt and
age information. Preserve origin-specific reporting, if useful, in events or
client bookkeeping rather than assigning different economic rights to
fungible cash. Adapt readers to an exact scaled net-balance view; do not
return invented held/unspent labels. Combining two separately rounded decays
can differ by one internal scaled unit per checkpoint; specify and test that
bound instead of promising identical rounding.

The old hot-path packing need not be abandoned. A candidate representation
is =uint200 netScaled=, =uint40 timestamp=, =uint16 flags= in one slot:
the existing account cap times Q fits in 199 bits. Carrying nominal value
needs additional storage, and the fund needs wider aggregate accounting.
Retain existing amount caps initially; never rely on a narrowing cast to check
them. This is a representation proposal, not a tested storage migration.
The signed BuckQty wrapper still used by credit/legacy code can be cleaned up
separately; the corrected token must not use a signed wallet balance.
Sponsorship requires additional funding state: do not count its protected
balance independently from the same money in its payer's funding pool.

For a Carrying service, retain nominal gross =G= alongside net =N=. Time
reduces N without reducing G. Transferring nominal =x= moves the proportional
net value, using one explicitly specified rounding rule; the recipient's mode
controls whether its nominal or net value is exposed by ERC-20 balanceOf.
Keep full-precision receipt evidence as well as nominal Transfer events.
Ordinary cash, Carrying nominal balances and NFT debt must never be collapsed
into one number. Synchronize registry mode changes before changing their
interpretation, and implement the selected neutral-service authorization.

** Jubilee cash and relief are separate computations

For an event-free interval with constant issued supply, reuse the tested fund
checkpoint, in scaled units:

#+begin_example
J_net(now) = S*Q - decay(S*Q - J_net(checkpoint), elapsed)
J_gross(now) = J_gross(checkpoint) + floor(S*Q * elapsed / T)
#+end_example

Checkpoint before changing S or transferring value into/out of fund cash.
Gross accrual includes self-demurrage; it is not another source of spendable
net BUCK. Deployed BUCK still pay demurrage in their actual holding accounts;
a basket position's mark is an asset claim, not additional token supply.
Reuse the full-width fund-flow counters and distinguish current cash from
lifetime incoming fees, investments, proceeds and paid relief.

Per-NFT issuance-seconds *remain necessary*. Each facility records principal
=P_i= and capped age =A_i <= P_i*T=. New issuance changes its weighted age;
partial retirement removes proportional age; ordinary transfers change
neither. Relief targets 2% of the *full issued principal* per year, even if
the issued BUCK have been spent or are left unspent in the wallet.

Reuse =JubileeClaims=/=JubileeClaimBook= for the exact aggregate capped age,
including deferred matured debt. A single integral of total supply cannot
substitute for the sum of separately capped ages after facilities mature.
Keep bounded maturity processing and explicit backlog refusal; do not
simplify the denominator by silently counting excess age.

For a selected reduction of principal p, use the selected capped age's share
of net fund cash plus the guarded wheel mark, capped at p. Baseline 2% is a
target; marked returns and losses determine actual relief. Normal reduction
and renewal use already-liquid full-value BUCK, with the existing operation
limit and shared block budget. A successful ordinary burn roughly costs the
owner =p - actual relief - net insurer refund=; exact rounding, coverage/K
constraints and the requested balance change determine the actual allocation.

At average issuance age 50 years, lien release remains available even if
cash is insufficient: Jubilee assumes the unburned principal, which stays in
supply until actually burned. The original insurer's nominal refund remains
an independently payable claim. Retain atomic in-place renewal, explicit
owner/insurer consent, premium-pool isolation and no worse K shortfall.

** Responsibilities and transition invariants

Coverage is backing, not currency. For example, activating 1,000 BUCK of
zero-premium self-insured coverage with zero spendable cash issues nothing;
a later 400-BUCK draw creates 400 BUCK of supply and 400 BUCK of aging NFT
principal. If activation instead costs a 100-BUCK premium, that premium is
real new principal delivered at full net value to the insurer's pool, even
when the owner requests zero spendable cash. Its reserve and premium bounds
still apply. Numeric mint/burn continue to mean actual balance changes.

| Block | Preconditions | Postconditions |
|-------+---------------+----------------|
| Checkpoint / transfer | Correct verified identities and receipts; authorized Carrying mode; sufficient sender net or nominal balance; safe time/amount range. | Money moves with its correct net/nominal value; cash never goes negative; neither NFT principal nor total issued supply changes; demurrage is counted once. |
| Mint / per-facility issue | NFT ownership and insurer consent; present-value/K capacity; exact premium terms and recorded pool; one pre-mint funding reserve for the whole batch. | Issued principal, supply, cash and premium funding increase consistently; each NFT and aggregate claim book synchronize; mint(N) increases balanceOf by at least N or all effects revert. |
| Coverage-only activation | Positive coverage; the same identity, ownership, maturity, premium and reserve checks as explicit issue. Any new premium principal fits K; free backing may improve an existing shortfall. | With zero premium, cash, principal, supply and claim age are unchanged. A nonzero premium is actual principal paid to the recorded insurer pool. An existing lien's age is never reset. |
| Burn / reduction | Owner-selected or documented default NFT order; exact current claim, pool refund, K and guarded mark; prepared insurer liquidity; current and shared Jubilee cash limits. | Owner net plus fund net extinguish exact principal; nominal refund comes from the original pool; coverage/age/supply decrease consistently; burn(N) achieves at least N of actual balance decrease or everything reverts. |
| Renewal / maturity | Correct facility and consent snapshot; normal renewal funding or the independent 50-year release eligibility. | Renewal crystallizes actual relief and updates one facility atomically; maturity releases the lien, retains unburned supply as fund debt, and preserves unpaid premium refund rights. |
| Sponsored fees | Two-party consent; verified ordinary accounts; no self-sponsorship/chains; actual payer funding, with projected spendability specified. | Sponsorship changes who bears the fee without creating net BUCK, double-counting payer money or changing NFT relief. Exhaustion, cancellation, fan-out and settlement order have explicit tested behavior. |
| Wheel investment / redemption | Existing wheel authority; one receipt; bounded work, guarded prices, nonzero meaningful execution bounds and actual liquidity. | Position, cash, shares and basis reconcile; actual proceeds determine liquidity; no position sale, swap or keeper reward executes under Buck's monetary lock. |
| Basket receipt / treasury exit | Position lock; actual activated backing covers principal under fresh K; requested shares and payout bounds are valid. | Principal and supply stay fixed; cash or in-kind delivery reconciles with retired shares, retained assets and fees. Final shares/basis and inventory still back principal, or every effect reverts. |

The conservation target is net BUCK across actual holding accounts equaling
issued supply within a proved rounding bound, and
=S = sum(open NFT principal) + deferred Jubilee principal=. A basket receipt
is not counted as BUCK a second time. Nominal Carrying balances must not be
summed as economic supply. New fund accrual must not be reconciled using the
old positive/negative balance and feesRealized/reliefRealized identities.

* Functional preservation and deliberate API corrections

| Surface | Action in the retained Buck |
|---------+-----------------------------|
| Metadata, ERC-20 approve/transfer/transferFrom/allowance, proof-bearing approve, identity checks, receipt fragments, public/private rules and allowance behavior | Keep the original public methods and identity dispatcher. Replace the money movement they call; reuse real proof/receipt regressions rather than assuming BuckNet facade equivalence. |
| Both mint and burn overloads, NFT order/default selectors, quoteMint/quoteBurn and Minted | Retain the existing transaction and quote shapes. Rewrite allocation/settlement for actual issuance, net refunds, per-NFT relief and final balance checks; retaining their signatures does not validate the old coverage-only inversion. =mint(max)= retains its defined maximum meaning; =burn(max)= is not a close-all sentinel. |
| K controller compute, premium reserve and live credit valuation | Preserve the existing controller and gates. Gate an entire mint on one starting reserve, excluding newly minted batch funds; distinguish capacity, principal and cash. Preserve no-worse-shortfall retirement without relaxing low-K exits. |
| Sponsorship request/accept/clear, isSponsored and settleDemurrage | Retain automatic fee offloading and a stable sponsored balanceOf. The latest rule is full sponsorship or revert: inadequate sponsor funding must revert settlement/spends and leave the linkage unchanged. Manual detachment remains independently available and applies ordinary outstanding fees to the account. Emit DemurrageSponsor(account, sponsor) on establishment and DemurrageSponsor(account, address(0)) on manual detachment. |
| insurancePool constructor argument/getter and insurer deposits/refunds | Preserve the existing argument's meaning; prefer an explicit additional Jubilee constructor argument over repurposing the fourth argument or introducing mutable wiring solely to retain its arity. Update deployment factories accordingly. New facilities must use an insurer-authorized pool; any bootstrap/default pool needs explicit insurer authorization, with no reassignment of old refunds. |
| raw/fee/fund views and flow events | Preserve useful observations with clearly documented net/nominal units. An ordinary net balance no longer has a separate unpaid gross-balance fee lien; do not imply it does or recreate one just to satisfy an old view. Inventory callers and explicitly document any revised/retired meaning. |
| signedBalanceOf/signedRawBalanceOf; holder-level reliefOf/redeemCost/settleRelief | Replace signed wallet/debt consumers with actual cash and per-NFT data. Holder-level automatic relief is the rejected model-branch addition, not a master feature that must be restored. Selected NFT retirement/renewal is authoritative. |
| setBasket/mintFromBasket/burnFromBasket | Keep existing legacy diagnostic coverage until replaced; do not restore the old unbacked hooks as production monetary functionality. Active baskets issue and retire through real BuckCredit facilities. |

Keeping Buck as the foundation does not preserve live legacy storage or
perform an automatic balance conversion. Do not retain obsolete slot layouts
merely because tests use vm.store; update those fixtures to meaningful setup
or an explicitly versioned harness. Source compatibility, economic semantics,
constructor wiring and deployed-state migration are distinct review items.

The November 1 proposal to replace legacy quotes with SDK-only helpers is
*not selected* by this new preservation path. Shared Rust/WASM/Python/JS may
provide richer estimates and policy decisions, but financial and identity
validation stays on chain. Existing transaction overloads still require an
on-chain allocation path. An eth_call preflight checks current execution but
neither reserves state nor returns allocation details from void mint/burn.

** Confirmed sponsorship: full payment or revert

The user's later November 4 simplification supersedes automatic failure
fallback. At settlement, compute the complete normal compound-decay fee since
the last successful checkpoint and settle the sponsor's own demurrage. If its
actual funds cover the entire fee, pay that whole amount and keep the account's
protected balance stable. Otherwise revert the whole operation with a typed
DemurrageSponsorUnderfunded error carrying the account, sponsor, required fee
and available net funds. Balances, checkpoints, flags and linkages roll back;
no partial sponsor payment, automatic self-charge or automatic disconnection.

The owner can fund the sponsor and retry, or explicitly detach. Manual
detachment clears the link *before* ordinary settlement, so it cannot be
trapped by the same insufficient-sponsor error. The account bears its complete
outstanding normal demurrage since its last successful checkpoint; earlier
successfully sponsored periods remain paid. Both parties retain the existing
right to end sponsorship. No fee is split or charged twice.

The canonical event is
=DemurrageSponsor(address indexed account, address indexed sponsor)=.
Establishment emits the actual pair; manual detachment emits
=(account, address(0))=. Failed settlements emit no lasting event. Existing
request/accept consent and payer events remain during consumer migration.

Unsettled sponsored displays are protected targets, not additional net
backing. Exact net views and conservation use the underlying declining cash;
settlement restores value only from actual sponsor cash. Other unsettled
sponsorship promises do not reserve that cash. With multiple dependants, an
earlier full payment may cause a later settlement to revert until funding or
explicit detachment. Test that ordering rather than silently allocating a
partial contribution across accounts.

Every spend settles before checking funds; an unfunded promise cannot be
spent. Explicit settleDemurrage also reverts while sponsorship is underfunded;
manual detachment, rather than another settlement, is the recovery path.
Test exact funding, one internal unit short, zero/same-block fees, multiple
dependants, voluntary clearing, identity/mode restrictions, and full rollback.
Fund accrual must not receive the same sponsored fee a second time.

Preserve the existing Buck behavior for Carrying receipts as well: the
sponsor must fund the inherited fee in full, or the entire receipt reverts,
including any earlier fee settlement in that transaction. Record the incoming
pool's actual net contribution separately from the sponsor's compensation;
never count the restored inherited fee as new fund income. Burn/refund quotes
must distinguish the pool's net outflow, the nominal refund and any additional
sponsor-funded value received by the owner. Net-output guards and receipt
reconciliation must also separate a payment from sponsorship restoration.

* Implementation sequence and checklist

** 1. Establish the retained contract's acceptance boundary

- [X] Compare master, model-branch Buck and current BuckNet; verify the Buck
  foundation is unchanged and create the isolated branch/worktree.
- [X] Record retained functionality, intentional signed-account corrections,
  donor components, responsibilities and transition invariants in this plan.
- [X] Classify existing behavioral tests: retain identity, allowance, NFT
  selection, funding, sponsorship and insurer checks; rewrite tests of linear
  demurrage, sign crossing, implicit repayment and old supply identities.
  Keep each distinct failure check or record its replacement before removal.
  The explicit transitional inventory below records tests not yet ported;
  compilation or a legacy-fixture pass does not complete their migration.
- [X] Confirm automatic stable-balance sponsorship and permission to detach
  an inadequately funded link at settlement; the user selected these rules.
- [X] Confirm full sponsorship or a reverting settlement/spend, with no
  automatic fallback; manual detachment remains independently available and
  charges outstanding ordinary demurrage to the account.
- [X] Implement full sponsorship or revert in the unsigned cash component
  and the retained Buck public API, including canonical events, rollback,
  funding-and-retry and unconditional manual detachment. Net backing remains
  observable independently in the component; its complete token integration
  is still part of the next monetary milestone.
- [X] Replace all 40 Buck string guards with 32 named errors and update
  preserved failure-path tests, including the actual reentrant revert bytes.
  Python/JS clients decode or match typed errors; full existing contract and
  wallet preservation checks pass. Monetary accounting remains legacy here.

** 2. Change Buck's accounting and monetary transitions together

- [X] Adapt =NetDemurrageMath= and the proven =BuckLedger= transitions into
  the unsigned account representation used by Buck. Collapse origin-only
  held/unspent tracking when reference comparisons justify it. Keep scaled
  remainders, long-idle behavior, mode-transition guards and explicit caps;
  test conservation/error bounds across splits, merges and frequent touches.
  BuckCash retains scaled remainders with one raw unit as the observed bound
  over the tested 32-operation randomized histories, not a universal proof.
- [X] Replace Buck's shared balance reads/writes, _crystallize, _feeOwing,
  _carryingTransfer, _debit/_credit, _setBalanceSigned and _accrueJubilee as
  one coherent accounting change. Remove supply updates from transfers.
  Modify =src/Buck.sol= itself; do not create a third token facade or simply
  rename BuckNet. Keep the original identity and allowance machinery.
- [X] Connect actual per-NFT issuance/reduction, insurer pool attribution,
  capped claim synchronization, full-value Jubilee payment and final mint/burn
  postconditions. Use tested pure mint/burn math as donor code while retaining
  Buck's selectors and public methods. Do not duplicate a second identity
  transfer implementation by importing the whole BuckNet orchestration layer.
- [-] Preserve/implement both quotes with execution-consistent rounding and
  a richer optional per-facility breakdown. Include all batch state that
  affects the next slice, including the shared budget remainder. The current
  availableReliefScaled view exposes only a one-outflow minimum, not the full
  budget snapshot. Distinguish work exhaustion from infeasibility. Both
  original aggregate quote signatures are implemented; the virtual burn batch
  includes shared pool balances/allowances, settled sponsor funding, claim age,
  fund cash and the exact block remainder. Richer public breakdown and the
  practical bounded-search availability issue remain.
- [X] Preserve funded sponsorship and implement approved Carrying facility
  owners. Fresh premiums must reach the insurer at full net value before any
  dilution into an old Carrying balance; refunds are nominal with the pool's
  average carried fee. Use truthful events and existing identity controls.
- [X] Decouple =JubileeFund= from the concrete BuckNet type through the narrow
  token interface it actually uses, rather than cloning the fund. Retain one
  fund, the claim book and renewal consent with verified immutable bindings.
  Specify the explicit fund constructor dependency, reuse the fund's existing
  authenticated one-time configuration and refuse monetary operations before
  complete wiring. Avoid a permanent dual-accounting mode.
- [X] Port renewal, 50-year lien release, original-pool deferred premium claims
  and deferred fund burns through Buck's guard and exact supply bookkeeping.
  Confirm failure in any callback or later batch slice rolls back the whole
  required atomic operation; maturity release retains its cash-cap exception.
  Existing real-fund renewal/maturity donor tests now run against canonical
  Buck and pass; a dedicated aged Carrying renewal test confirms that the
  replacement premium reaches the original/new insurer pool at full net value.

** Transitional test inventory and deletion boundary

=src/legacy/BuckLinear.sol= freezes =bb35ef68='s old accounting solely for
explicit legacy fixtures and the pre-existing unbacked basket-hook diagnostic.
It is not an alternate mode of Buck, is never selected by the corrected token,
and must not become a fallback in current economy builders. Twenty-one old
test imports explicitly name it as =BuckLinear as Buck= and contain a legacy
warning. The old Notes bootstrap script is also explicitly legacy until its
separate proof/deployment milestone. Remove these references as the following
distinct checks are demonstrated against Buck, rather than deleting failures
or claiming the old test count as current-token validation.

Canonical issuance/retirement tests and existing independent integer planner vectors replace monetary assertions; add complete donor mint/burn compatibility and marked renewal coverage. |

BuckCash, BuckCompound and exhaustive BuckBurnModes test unsigned compounded cash, explicit debt, fund self-demurrage, nominal Carrying value and exact per-NFT age. No old slot layout is imposed on Buck. |

Preserve A1/A2/B1, nullifier, domain, replay and rollback checks; regenerate real proofs explicitly. Current legacy successes are not corrected-token proof evidence. |

The legacy equity/desk suites now share abstract =BuckBasketEquityFixture=
and =BasketDeskFixture= setup, rather than inheriting executable tests.
The desk was idle during the inherited equity tests; machine setup occurs
only in its own tests. Those repetitions exercised the same unchanged paths.
Interaction checks remain explicit desk tests and stateful invariants.

| Suite | Before | After | Distinct coverage retained |
|-------+--------+-------+----------------------------|
| BuckBasketEquityTest | 29 | 29 | Original standalone equity suite |
| BasketDeskTest | 35 | 6 | Own issuance, grants, relief and depositor separation |

The five suites pass all 50 cases. Before/after ABI inventories preserve the
exact distinct-name union and each suite's intended cases. Fingerprints of
all 51 compiled distributed contracts preserve creation/runtime bytecode,
ABI and library links. The full regression and complete package digest are
recorded in the milestone log; neither an economic scenario nor any
canonical Buck acceptance test is removed by this consolidation.

Current canonical monetary code is split by responsibility: Buck owns the
original external identity/allowance/sponsorship surface and lock; BuckCash
owns cash/supply; BuckTransfer reuses the original identity dispatcher for all
payments; BuckFacilities owns allocation/settlement; BuckRenewalOps owns atomic
renewal. They share one book and one external fund, not parallel token modes.
The pure BuckNetMintMath/BuckNetBurnMath names temporarily identify reused
mathematical primitives, not deployment of the BuckNet facade.

Fresh issuance sends spendable BUCK to the owner and premium BUCK directly
to the insurer, with truthful zero-address Transfer events and an owner/pool
identity receipt. This prevents an old Carrying owner's average fee from
underfunding a fresh premium. Standard transfer remains nominal for Carrying
services. An internal full-value renewal payment additionally emits
FullValuePayment with the exact consumed nominal basis; it deliberately pays
its own carried fee rather than imposing it on the insurer. CashRetired
separates exact principal/net destruction from visible nominal burn events.

The existing pure burn planner demonstrates non-monotone rounding: retiring
four raw units can debit four, while five debits only three after relief and
refund. K eligibility can accept one, reject two and accept three. A real
3,438-raw-unit case exhausts 256 search steps although a full retirement is
valid. Preserve these regressions and improve practical gas/availability;
retaining the source foundation does not solve the allocation problem.
The prototype's sampled maximum 1,275,392 burn gas is not a worst-case bound.

** 3. Validate complete Buck functionality and reuse one basket wheel

- [-] Exercise the retained Buck through mint, aging, an identity-checked
  Carrying-pool transfer, purchase, selected NFT burn and full rollback, with
  nonzero premium, multiple NFTs/insurers and changed K. Add real private and
  public identity combinations, invalid/wrong-domain proofs, receipt hashes,
  finite/infinite allowance, zero/self transfers and authorized mode changes.
  Canonical contract, Python/JS and real-identity wallet checks cover the
  monetary/receipt path. Keep explicit remaining domain, provenance and
  release acceptance rather than treating legacy fixture counts as coverage.
- [X] Transfer donor tests for long-idle compound decay, exact per-NFT age,
  mixed-age issuance, fund self-demurrage, marked gain/loss, both liquidity
  guards, shared cross-path budgets, renewal, maturity/deferred supply and
  insurer liquidity. Run against corrected Buck, not merely against BuckNet.
- [-] Use the existing valuation-only wheel refresh at burn start. Guard its
  basket registration, source, position key, freshness and spot/TWAP bounds;
  no swaps, basket sales or wheel reward may execute under Buck's lock. Remove
  the prototype's no-wheel live-mark fallback at production wiring.
- [X] Reuse the existing one-position fund investment/redemption, share/basis
  interface and wheel. Add bounded scheduled liquidity work to that wheel,
  with authentic routes/minimums, keeper authority and fair bounded scans.
  The current cash target is 10% of the capped benchmark, a one-day half-life
  on observed excess, and a 1%-of-target minimum investment. It is separate
  from the 10% payment limit. A later block does not itself run the wheel.
  JubileeKind adds three authenticated jobs to both existing wheels, with
  bounded heap processing, capped deferred burns and positive-bound reserve
  trades. All target reads/execution are caught per job, failed trades roll
  back, and a job is attempted only once per block. Cash discovery can
  checkpoint without earning a reward; otherwise idle economies never start
  their reserve budget. Mutating scans/funding reject callback reentry while
  valuation publication remains independent. Primary builders must still
  configure the same wheel/fund/basket bindings explicitly.
- [X] Update the existing basket/desk to explicit principal/cash accounting,
  actual facility issue/retirement and unchanged low-K solvency requirements.
  Canonical mode now supports the governed public openCredit API. Resolve
  basket/Jubilee cross-holding valuation
  using existing marks without recursion, dilution or double counting. Test
  entry/exit, gains/losses, singular cases, stale marks and bounded real sales.
  Approved November 5: claims enter LP pricing, not new-credit collateral.
  The capped, fee-aware integer solver and independent exhaustive tests are
  complete. Canonical basket snapshots now read actual cash, actual NFT
  principal and direct fund receipt ownership. LP prices include the solved
  claim; credit marks exclude it and preserve the separate held-BUCK offset.
  Harness probes exercise funding boundaries; the unmodified shell also
  passes public opening, actual V3 issuance, explicit retirement and investor
  exit through both upkeep facets. Production credential/binding checks
  separately exercise the public ordinary identity and matched NFT setup.
  Legacy branches remain for unconverted fixtures; primary-world wiring is
  part of step 4, not evidence supplied by these contract tests.
  BasketJubileeMath is one linked read-only valuation library shared by the
  shell and existing facets. No new wheel, fund or token mode was introduced.
  Constituent registration now runs in the existing venue facet through the
  governed shell wrapper and authenticated self-call. Moving this AMM setup
  out of the share/credit shell retains its rules and frees runtime space:
  shell 23,833 bytes at the earlier bounded-retirement checkpoint, V3 venue
  20,603, machine venue 20,915. Current runtime sizes are in the latest log.
  Retain the explicit
  runtime test and use the existing facet structure for further substantial code.
- [ ] Complete the approved Carrying-service allowlist, safe revocation and
  settlement access; production insurer preparation and strategies; gas,
  runtime/creation limits and library/callback provenance. BuckCredit's last
  measured runtime was 24,516 bytes against the 24,576-byte runtime limit.
  Canonical wheel identity binding and funded reward acceptance are now
  implemented below. Cash-only platform acceptance still does not configure
  that role in the primary world. Canonical Carrying-basket inventory/fee
  acceptance and primary arb activation are recorded below. The older
  comparison wheel has very little runtime space: prefer the canonical machine wheel
  in primary builders and retire legacy variants only after mapping their
  distinct detection coverage. This binding milestone does not complete the
  ecosystem-wide service allowlist or production insurer strategies.
- [ ] Keep BuckNet as an explicitly experimental regression oracle only until
  its distinct checks are exercised against Buck, then retire duplicate token
  orchestration and diagnostic deployment paths as coverage is demonstrated.
  Do not discard the independent mathematical reference or useful primitives.

Normal relief obeys both a current-cash operation cap and one shared block
budget. The first positive protected outflow snapshots 10% of then-current
net fund cash; reductions, renewals and deferred burns share its remainder.
Later same-block inflows do not refill it. Zero/failed operations do not start
or consume it. Marks do not promise realizable liquidity; retain borrower
minimum-relief/principal bounds where provided and reject stale/unsafe inputs.

This expands the partially completed basket/desk item above; it is the next
implementation sequence, before changing the primary economy builder.

  A missing relief mark does not inflate or block bare credit collateral.
