* Next economic integration boundary

** Calendar implementation acceptance

Keep the existing household and its actual/counterfactual books. Separate
calendar accrual from outgoing monetary operations: an uncertain transaction
must not silently stop mortgage interest, wages, expenses or the mortgage
counterexample's clock. Money becomes spendable only from actual balances;
never credit an unconfirmed mint or payment as liquid funds.

- [X] Define one simulation-day/receipt-time mapping, using the explicit
  bank compounding and pay-period convention consistently in both languages.
  Keep the model's clock and the receipt's effective payment date explicit.
  Both Python runners now distribute ticks with absolute integer timestamps;
  JS DayClock absorbs intervening wheel/transaction work. Shared epoch,
  different arrivals and interleaved work have actual-contract coverage;
  pure five-year clock checks include non-divisor tick counts and overrun.
- [X] Maintain a dated external-debt payment history or equivalent
  recoverable checkpoints. A bank receipt discovered late reduced the loan
  at its confirmed effective date, not at the later recovery date. Reconcile
  interest from that point; do not overcharge interest on already retired
  principal. Preserve original shadow-mortgage terms and schedule separately.
- [X] Queue each payday/expense period once. Checkpoint the simulated USDC
  wage mint and authenticate its actual zero-address transfer, then exact
  living/insurance/mortgage payments with their saved allocations. Reuse
  the existing settlement evidence helpers where possible. Known hashes
  reconcile once; unknown hashes cannot authorize another mint/payment.
- [X] Decide explicitly how unconfirmed income and delayed bills appear in
  personal reports. Preserve scheduled accrual and expose incomplete or
  provisional comparisons; do not fabricate spendable cash or quietly move
  the counterfactual's payment dates to match an RPC outage.
- [X] Serialize discretionary owner operations with the existing pending
  machine. Test an interrupted monthly phase and an interrupted refinance
  across actual elapsed EVM days, in both languages, against an independent
  debt/cash-flow oracle. Recovery must neither mint another salary nor pay
  the same mortgage allocation twice.
  Actual Python/JS interruptions across real elapsed days and a payday pass,
  including dated refinance recovery and a known waiting buyback target.
  Overall primary acceptance remains separately tracked in the handoff.
- [X] Keep external premiums proportional to actual activated coverage and
  protect the issuance reserve in subsequent saving/paydown decisions.
  Apply closing costs once to a coherent staged mortgage refinance;
  distinguish actual insurer activation from a closing cost already paid.
  Proportional recurring premiums, staged closing and reserve-safe surplus
  are implemented and tested; the surplus checkpoint records the last.
- [X] Separate actual insurance activation from confirmed mortgage closing.
  Only an authenticated issuance-backed bank allocation sets refinance_closed;
  economic quotes retain the fixed fee before that point. Wallet-only
  prepayment does not switch insurance; later tranches cannot pay a second
  closing fee. Public histories/personal records expose the distinction.
- [X] Retain a refused bank leg as payment_due and expose bank-only retry.
  Reuse its saved allocation and original cash buffer; never repeat its
  confirmed issue/export. Block a new refinance while that obligation waits.
  Unknown outcomes stay pending, and dated/calendar/buyback serialization
  still applies. A smaller remaining mortgage requires reassessment.
- [X] Assess an affordable staged programme at the current K/credit and a
  conservative per-tranche conversion price. A liquidity-limited first leg
  need not repay the entire startup fee by itself; pay the actual first fee
  in full and require both programme admission and worthwhile marginal legs.
  Do not count future wages, higher K or unquoted insurance capacity as funds.
  Reuse the same five size fractions and actual coverage/refund menus. Only
  after standalone first-tranche choices fail, score a larger same-asset
  programme at today's conservative bounded-trade conversion price. It must
  pass with the full startup cost and fit current cash/reserve/penalty bounds.
  Allocate startup cost by principal for the marginal leg's economic score;
  its actual first bank payment still includes the entire fee. Prefer the
  largest worthwhile current principal; never issue the forecast amount at
  once. Context labels the assumption of route repair between tranches;
  every later operation needs fresh prices/credit and can decline.
- [X] Keep refused post-issuance settlement explicit. settlement_due covers
  export and mortgage refusal; payment_due identifies the bank-only retry.
  A new assessment/loan cannot erase the old export and manufacture a free
  wallet-only closing or duplicate existing issuance.
- [X] Complete explicit export resumption/reassessment after a proved refusal,
  including reduced remaining mortgage allocations. Preserve actual funded
  cash and issuance and their original closing obligation; no replayed mint
  or artificial disposal of the remaining BUCKs. Unknown outcomes remain
  pending. This must precede the automatic dispatcher, not be bypassed by it.
  Implementation now reuses the same receipt machine. The original plan
  retains its caps; a revision holds the new unfinished cash bounds for
  receipt authentication. Reassessments quote current Ordinary cash and
  conversion capacity without another facility/fund/insurer quote. Prior
  premium, insurance saving and relief are already in the books; only the
  marginal wallet trade is scored, while the full committed closing fee stays
  in its bank cash budget. A bank-only remainder uses existing USDC and no
  market quote. Zero principal can pay a due fee; zero total finishes without
  a transfer or repayment count. Same-owner concurrent attempts recheck the
  revision before dispatch. Shared actual Python and JS qualification is
  complete, including lost replies, waits, zero/fee-only completion and
  concurrent JS triggers; results are recorded in the current handoff.
- [X] Add an exact integer local sale-input estimator to both SDKs, using
  the same units as the existing forward quote. Independent binary-search
  vectors cover aged/heavily fee-carrying pools, dust, full-width values and
  exhausted output/input bounds. Inversion avoids overselling BUCK when the
  remaining external mortgage has become smaller.

** Remaining economy sequence

*** November 8 priority: complete minimal ecosystem, then five years

This order supersedes older sequencing that requires full save/resume or
the legacy control lab's eleven cumulative stages before running an economy.
Keep one canonical Python runtime for the savings server and batch runner,
and the matching existing JavaScript world. Reuse their households, calendar,
markets, work wheel and reporting; do not port a second legacy economy engine.

**** Carrying basket is part of the arb milestone (owner instruction)

The canonical basket is to be an approved *Carrying service*, rather than
an Ordinary wallet. This supersedes the former Ordinary-only basket setup
and the initial funded prototype's Ordinary wallet assumption. It does not
change household wallets, the 2% demurrage rule, actual NFT principal, K,
the Jubilee cash cap or the rule excluding unpaid claims from new credit.

- [X] Centralize net/nominal cash and market operations in the same shared
  linked helper used by funded arb. Keep existing venue/ERC-20 amounts
  nominal; keep policy budgets, LP shares/ROI, reserves, collateral and
  redemption minimums net. A gross nominal balance is never shareholder
  wealth or full-value credit backing. Avoid separate Ordinary/Carrying
  basket implementations and duplicated market engines.
- [X] Permit verified, approved Carrying basket credit owners in the existing
  setup and bind fresh canonical Python/JS baskets Carrying before approvals
  freeze their mode. Reuse Buck.setApprovedCarryingIssuer and existing
  repayment/renewal mechanisms. No new Buck monetary mode is required.
- [X] Normalize swaps, LP mint/burn, income collection and in-kind/cash exits.
  Gross up nominal payouts when necessary to deliver promised net value,
  measure actual changes, and retain net reserve/K postconditions. Include
  fractional/dust and heavily aged inventory; newly issued fresh cash changes
  the basket's average fee basis rather than creating a selectable lot.
- [X] Update funded sizing/guard to track the Carrying basket's own pooled
  gross/net balances alongside the pools'. Measure combined net inventory
  and LP return at frozen pre-trade marks, including actual new LP fees once.
  Accept funded direct cash losses when combined net return is positive;
  caller rewards remain secondary.
- [X] Observe actual high-fee BUCK leaving an aged TOKEN/BUCK pool, entering
  the Carrying basket and later the fresher BUCK/USDC pool. Expose nominal
  transfer, delivered net and all three before/after fee ratios. Turnover is
  a hypothesis for a fresher USDC pool, not a guaranteed fee ordering.
- [X] Replace the attempted special direct pool-to-pool routing workaround
  with ordinary sequential routing through the Carrying basket. Its outgoing
  BUCK carries its pooled average fee basis; do not pretend a particular
  incoming BUCK lot can be selected or its age reset.
- [X] Qualify both actual facets/wheel and Python/JS fresh deployment, net
  deposits/redemptions, Jubilee single receipt and household quotes. Existing
  live/frozen Ordinary identities cannot simply change flavor; use fresh sim
  worlds and a separately reviewed real deployment migration.

The contract implementation is committed at =9e0364d3=. The subsequent
focused Carrying/arb set passes 53, including aged net-payout fuzzing,
mixed-age fractional nominal debit and reserve-deficit repair. Both existing
wheels and facets fit the runtime limit. Python bootstrap/marks/short JSON/PDF
integration passes six checks, now including active shock repair. The
corrected complete JS household acceptance passes 41 checks; the fresh
active-arb world separately passes its actual withdrawal/repair test.
Canonical Python/JS machine worlds enable arb by default, with explicit
disable switches for component comparisons. See the current handoff for
final logs and the remaining household/five-year boundary.

- [X] First canonical arb qualification: real V3, actual NFT-backed public
  basket LP placement, BUCK/USDC shocks in both directions, no caller reward,
  actual LP fee ownership, protocol fee exclusion and aged net collection.
  Repair callback scope after reproducing a pool retaining authority after
  the outermost swap. Characterize nominally unchanged wheel BUCK inventory
  losing net backing during an otherwise TOKEN-profitable cycle.
- [X] Complete net-portfolio/funding qualification and enable the existing
  wheel's arb against canonical Buck and real V3. Cover TOKEN moves,
  existing inventory and both directions. Extend the same bounded
  engine to all simple triangles/four-pool cycles. Read current liquidity,
  fees and net/gross balances during normal ticks; never trade inside the
  read-only valuation path used by Buck.
- [X] Keep runtime work bounded without adding another scheduler: at most
  four examined slots/one completed job per transaction, real cursor progress
  through one rotation or the caller's work budget, and publication between
  batches. Run arb before Ops/response capital deployment. Preserve actual
  confirmed receipt prefixes rather than synthesize a combined receipt.
- [X] Permit atomic use of the basket's own cash while preserving its
  pre-trade net reserve floor, or never worsening an existing reserve deficit.
  Prohibiting initial spends from reserve would starve useful repair work.
  Do not finance arb by issuance, principal sales or caller reward reserves.
- [X] Include actual basket LP fee ownership, protocol fees and carried-fee
  exchange in the cost/return model. An exchange can replace higher-fee
  BUCK with lower-fee BUCK or TOKEN exposure that a nominal-price-only
  external searcher misses. Use combined net portfolio improvement,
  without double-counting LP income or funding cash losses with uncollected
  fees. Maximize basket ROI first; caller rewards and direct cash surplus
  are secondary. Do not use the ability to pay a caller as an arb eligibility
  requirement. Funded settlement remains bounded separately. The detailed
  invariants are in [[file:../BASKET-WHEEL.org::*Current accounting and implementation direction (2026-11-08)][BASKET-WHEEL.org]].
- [ ] Finish autonomous household decisions in both languages: transfer as
  much external mortgage principal as practical into actual BUCK issuance,
  subject to K, conversion liquidity, buffers and whole-programme costs.
  A household already committed to refinancing should not wait an arbitrary
  new adoption delay between every affordable tranche. Retain heterogeneous
  adoption/price/risk thresholds and adaptive surplus saving/paydown.
- [X] Implement the automatic debtor refinancing component on the existing
  owner dispatcher. Recover previous receipts and buybacks, process healthy

  Preserve keyed pre-adoption phase/cadence; actual issuers seek a fresh
  bounded tranche daily. Limit new assessment/resumption to once per day;
  uncertain evidence blocks later sends and recovered uncertainty consumes
  the call. Expose next/last attempt days and declined/unavailable histories.
  Python and matching actual JS qualification pass. The first JS chronology
  exposed an exact-balance export boundary after a later adoption; the
  corrected owned-cash sizing passes the timed case and full chronology.
  This completes the component, not saver/surplus/working-money/exits or the
  full normal act gate.
- [X] Retain owned execution cash above the protected reserve in fresh plans,
  reusing the same 0.1% sizing helper as refused-export reassessment. Funding,
  issuance and sale are separate transactions; compounding continues between

  the smaller sale. Forecast the full liability conservatively and report
  unexported cash in actual marks. Expose projected post-export cash/cushion.
  A one-minute actual Python reproducer failed before this fix with a
  0.000197 BUCK shortfall; it now closes with the original reserve intact.
  Matching actual JS qualification passes, including the one-minute delay,

*** Next household integration after calendar qualification

Use the existing household, dispatcher and runner. Calendar completion alone
does not restore the normal decision loop. Keep these remaining behaviors
explicit so simplification does not remove an economic source of demand:

| Existing path | Required canonical successor | Simplification boundary |
|---------------+------------------------------+-------------------------|
| Floating monthly/advance and direct payments | Dated calendar, authenticated income/bills and independent shadow books | After the calendar is qualified, move the old insurance-cost fixture onto it and make the old path legacy-only. Its separate working-wallet action still needs migration. |
| Working-wallet payday action | Guarded net purchases/sales from surplus after actual bills and buffers; preserve distinct custody | *Done as a component* (working-money checkpoint): one capped trade a pay period on the shared receipt cursor, the wallet in its own Ordinary account. The legacy monthly caller stays legacy-only; the calendar still submits no discretionary trade. |
| Debtor refinancing versus saver credit investment | Reuse selected issuance, reserve funding, bounded market operations and one owner receipt dispatcher; distinguish bank payment from investment/cash retention | Extend common execution where it is the same operation. A saver has no mortgage, so it must not manufacture a positive bank allocation to pass the refinance plan's debt bounds or duplicate the whole refinance machine. |
| First closing versus later tranches | Confirmed one-time closing plus current-credit/capital staged admission now implemented | Activation alone does not pay a fee. Allocate it only for economics, pay it fully on the first bank receipt, and recheck every later tranche. Resolve refused export prefixes before automatic scheduling. |
| Surplus saving and discounted retirement | Protect required issuance cash/reserve, quote full net acquired BUCK including pool-carried fees, then authenticate actual deposit or selected settlement | *Done as components* (surplus checkpoint). A nominal pool discount alone is insufficient, and neither is one dislocated dollar route. Targets survive actual uncertainty; known waiting intentions coexist with normal income and bills. |
| Asset exits | Explicit unused-facility close, guarded principal retirement and receipt redemption | Keep K/fund limits intact. The unused-facility close is =burn(0)= (owner decision 2026-11-12, superseding "keep burn(0) invalid"; implemented, release milestone). Preserve distinct exit checks as household integration proceeds. |
| Saved-world state (later) | Dated books/queues/hashes/cursors and exact fund/insurer graph | Keep imports gated until restart coverage demonstrates the replacement; save/resume does not gate uninterrupted economy runs. |

*** Economy acceptance sequence

The user approved the pricing/collateral policy on November 5: include marked
Jubilee claims in LP receipt pricing through the capped mutual-holding
calculation; exclude unpaid claims from collateral for new issuance. The
[[file:basket-jubilee-crossholding.org][cross-holding note]] gives the equation, dilution counterexample and rounding
policy. A mark is not liquid relief and cannot relax K or fund outflow limits.

Continue in this order:

1. Convert the existing basket/desk snapshots to actual cash and NFT principal;
   resolve the chosen mark without recursive fund/basket calls. Keep conservative
   K solvency and low-K exits. Replace implicit draw/repay with explicit issuance
   and reduction, and connect the existing wheel's marked reserve work.
   Basket valuation is connected and tested through actual debt, with harness
   boundary probes and the unmodified shell's public lifecycle. Explicit wheel issuance and bounded retirement are
   tested, as are actual-collateral cash/in-kind exits and the separate desk's
   canonical issue/purchase/retirement paths. Price-unit vectors distinguish
   nominal issuance from net liquidation. Fund reserve and maturity jobs are
   now connected to both existing wheels, including optional basket principal
   scheduling. Public identity/credit onboarding and canonical wheel binding /
   private-keeper rewards now pass. The existing JS bootstrap now wires the
   canonical graph and real opening pools. Python now does likewise, including
   its separate desk, without disabled comparison advisers. Canonical Python
   observations and a short bootstrap-only JSON/PDF run now pass; personal
   Python/JS reports also pass. Complete household actions remain open.
   Canonical funded arbitrage is now enabled and primary-shock-qualified,
   including actual LP ownership and carried-fee inventory. Receiving
   cash alone must not reduce principal or make an insolvent
   low-K exit pass. Keep fund liquidity preparation outside the position lock.
2. Finish the existing Python/JS household lifecycle and primary world builders;
   retain adaptive default and the explicit comparison policies. Port setup,
   issuance, asset exits, basket redemption, personal marks and persistent
   outcomes rather than adding another economy runner.
   Household setup and same-block selected-facility books now work in both
   languages, including complete personal portfolio marks; normal decisions
   remain explicitly gated. Primary guarded-market/repayment adapters and
   exact-payment primitives now pass in both languages. Explicit assessed
   plans now use bounded actual economic selection and confirmed bank
   payments. Observed defaults now feed assessment; finish receipt-safe
   calendar/surplus scheduling and exits before enabling normal decisions.
3. Run identical operation vectors and a short real-contract household world,
   then the five-year baselines/policy comparisons and retained shocks. Save
   raw JSON, adjacent PDFs and campaign metadata for every completed long run.
4. Restore the Notes accounting fixtures and real generated A1/A2/B1 proof
   suites against the same canonical artifact, and finish release/provenance
   gates. No new five-year run or corrected-token Notes acceptance is claimed
   by the wallet smoke tests.

Also cover voluntary release of a zero-principal facility with remaining
activated coverage, particularly after a fully relieved renewal. Numeric
burn(0) must not become an undocumented NFT-close sentinel; ensure the owner
can release an unused lien and recover any original-pool premium without
inventing or extinguishing principal.

/Superseded 2026-11-12./ The owner decided that =burn(0)= /is/ that release,
documented rather than a hidden sentinel: it releases all unused coverage,
and excess coverage on a facility that still has principal, refunding the
premium deposit in proportion. It still neither invents nor extinguishes
principal, and it does not close the NFT. Implemented 2026-11-12; see the
release milestone at the end of this file.

* Reference map and verification commands

[[file:net-demurrage-invariants.org][The BuckNet invariant map]] describes the experimental donor, not a mandate
to retain its facade or its held/unspent split. The [[file:demurrage-implementation-plan.org][historical contract record]],
[[file:net-demurrage-simulation-migration.org][simulation map]] and [[file:net-demurrage-interface-migration.org][core/Notes/proof map]] retain implementation detail;
this document owns execution order. The [[file:credit-facility-accounting-design.org][facility accounting design]],
[[file:basket-jubilee-crossholding.org][cross-holding analysis]] and [[file:jubilee-marked-relief.org][marked-relief analysis]] supply the detailed reasoning.
Reconcile conflicts with this plan and the user's decisions.

Use [[file:../../README-build.org][the build guide]], [[file:../../README-simulations.org][simulation guide]] and [[file:../../README-sandboxes.org][sandbox guide]] and the existing Make wrappers.
These commands already exist; donor test names do not imply that Buck has
been corrected. A fresh worktree requires its own documented dependencies;
do not share JS node_modules or trust another worktree's unverified library.

  Arb slots precede Ops/response capital deployment in the same wheel.

*** Surplus saving and retirement checkpoint

- *Worktree/branch*: =.claude/worktrees/buck-compound= on
  =feature/household-minimal-ecosystem=. No Solidity, demurrage kernel, K
  rule or Jubilee allowance changed. Normal household act is still gated and
  no five-year campaign has been started.
- *Handoff failure 1, the exit preview* (resolved). The direct contract-owner
  =eth_call= was refused by the *backend*, not by the exit: pyrevm 0.3.7
  rejects any message whose sender has code before running it (EIP-3607,
  =Transaction(RejectCallerWithCode)=) and cannot waive that for calls as
  anvil does. The backend had reported it as an empty revert; it now says
  =not executed: Transaction(...)=. The same =redeemShares= called through
  the household proxy's own =SimLP.exec= returns the real values and changes
  neither holdings, cash, K nor chain height. =NetBasketOps= / =netBasketOps=
  take an optional =simulate= (read-only twin of =execute=); both sim worlds
  supply it (=d.net_simulate=, =world.netMarket.simulate=). The default
  remains a direct call from the owner. No contract or guard changed, and the
  executed redemption pays exactly the previewed amount in the same state.
- *Handoff failure 2, the retirement signal* (resolved; the policy was right,
  the fixture was incoherent). Fair value is the basket's BUCK price times
  the /same/ dollar pool's spot, so that pool's price cancels:
  =cost/fair = (pool nominal/net) / ((1 - fee) * BVIB)=. The fixture sold
  100,000 BUCK into BUCK/USDC only: spot fell 1.0000 -> 0.8265 while BVIB
  stayed 0.999924, so the ratio stayed 1.0007 and adaptive correctly kept
  saving. This is the long-standing household parity reference (legacy
  =_basket_usd=), not a new choice; it is now stated in the assessor and
  guide, and =fair_basis= is recorded with each choice. The fixtures keep the
  isolated sale as a /negative/ assertion and then complete a coherent
  sell-off (the same share of every TOKEN pool), which the basket's TWAP
  carries into fair value.
- *Handoff item 3, two reserves* (resolved). The refinance assessor's default
  reserve was the previous flow's cursor, so after a surplus flow it was
  /all prior wallet BUCK/. A household-level =_net_credit_reserve= /
  =netCreditReserve= now holds the lasting issuance front (set by each
  started refinance plan); a surplus plan's floors are reported separately as
  =surplus_protection= and end with that operation. The interleave fixture
  shows the old default would have frozen all but the save's 0.1% sizing
  cushion (about 5 BUCK of 500) out of a later wallet prepayment.
- *Retirement in the public history*. A retire plan's completion is
  =outcome=retirement-funded= with principal unchanged. The selected-facility
  engine appends =kind=retirement= records: one =confirmed= per authenticated
  =FacilityReduced= receipt (exact hash, facility, principal, relief, premium
  refund), plus =pending=, =waiting=, =refused=, =recovered=, =canceled= and
  =complete=. =status()= exposes the retained =retirement= target, reserve
  and uncertainty and the lasting =credit_reserve=. A settled reduction now
  names its facility in both SDKs. Recording is non-fatal and follows the
  saved books and pending evidence. Surplus flows no longer report
  refinance-named pending messages.
- *Shared outcomes* (=test/fixtures/household-surplus.json=, =flow=): twelve
  cases over purchase, deposit and redemption. A lost reply is authenticated
  from its saved hash and applied once; an unknown outcome books and re-sends
  nothing and also holds the next payday; a proved refusal of a purchase or
  redemption changes nothing; a refused or unquotable deposit keeps the
  purchased cash and retries only the deposit; a refused exit preview falls
  back to a guarded purchase; a fund refusal keeps the funded target. A
  refusal is the same send with one bound made unreachable, so the contract
  itself reverts.
- *A regression found outside the focused subsets*: =test_net_market.py=
  (part of =make nix-venv-net-demurrage-smoke=) had failed since =acffbc25=
  because its bare household never defined =refinance_closed=. The fixture is
  corrected; the lesson is in the recommendations below.
- *Evidence at this checkpoint* (final sources; counts, not log paths):
  - Python, =make nix-venv-test-python= over the sixteen supported files
    (the economy-accounting list, the net-demurrage-smoke list and
    =test_net_economy_bootstrap.py=): *258 passed*, no skips, 82 s. That
    includes seventeen surplus cases: both lifecycle arms, the twelve shared
    flow cases, the interleave, the funding target and the SDK basket
    operations.
  - Fast JavaScript, =make nix-core-equilibrium-policy-test=: *78 passed*.
  - Actual JavaScript surplus fixture
    (=test/net-household-surplus.tevm.test.js=, one Tevm world): *33 Node
    checks passed* (32 children and the parent), no skips, about nineteen
    minutes. The children are the interleave, the twelve flow cases as an
    attempt and a next day, the in-place top-up, the isolated-route check,
    both lifecycle arms, the SDK basket operations and the payday hold.
  - JavaScript repayment-target and market-record units: *6 passed*.
  - The browser equilibrium bundle rebuilds (=make nix-core-demo-eqworld=).
  - Full actual JavaScript equilibrium acceptance
    (=make nix-core-equilibrium-bootstrap-test=, all five files run
    together): *88 passed*, no skips, about thirty minutes. The earlier arb,
    bootstrap, programme and resume fixtures are unchanged and still pass on
    the shared household machine.
  - =make nix-venv-net-demurrage-smoke= (the repayment engine's Python and
    JavaScript fixtures): *35 Python and 20 JavaScript passed*, no skips.
  The fixture is in the existing Make target's default file list. The
  preceding checkpoints' counts (190 Python and so on) belong to their own
  revisions and are not restated here.
- *Commands*:
  #+begin_src sh
  make nix-core-equilibrium-policy-test
  make nix-venv-test-python PYTEST_FILES=alberta_buck/test/test_net_household_setup.py PYTEST_ARGS='-k "surplus or saving_between or funding_target or primary_basket_ops" -q'
  make nix-core-equilibrium-bootstrap-test CORE_EQUILIBRIUM_TEST_FILES=test/net-household-surplus.tevm.test.js
  #+end_src
  Temporary logs are diagnostic, not durable acceptance artifacts; commands,
  vectors, tests and this status are committed.

*** Observations and recommendations for the five-year runs and sandboxes

Recorded at the end of the surplus phase. /Measured/ items name their
fixture; none is a five-year result. /Recommendations/ are for the remaining
milestones (working wallet, saver credit investment, asset exits, the normal
act loop, short worlds, the five-year campaigns, the savings sandbox and the
JavaScript equilibrium demo).

**** Measured in this phase

Fixture A is the Python =household_world= (pyrevm, two TOKENs, a 1M/1M dollar
pool, 100k-BUCK TOKEN pools, an un-bootstrapped basket). Fixture B adds two
bootstrap seeders and wheel turns (basket lien 499,961 BUCK; 350k-BUCK TOKEN
pools). Fixture C is the actual JavaScript builder under Tevm (1M dollar pool,
a bootstrapped leveraged basket, two TOKENs).

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 1 | A | A 10% BUCK sale into the dollar pool alone: spot 1.0000 -> 0.8265, BVIB unchanged at 0.999924, cost/fair 1.0007. No household retirement signal, by construction. |
| 2 | A | The same 10% in every BUCK pool: BVIB 1.2093 once the TWAP passes, cost/fair 0.8275, full retirement chosen. K fell 0.7500 -> 0.7262 at the first compute, then about 0.0034 a day (0.7222, 0.7189, 0.7155, 0.7121) while nothing repaired the pools. |
| 3 | A | Wheel turns deploying about 5.5k BUCK of household savings into the 100k TOKEN pools raised BVIB about 0.5% a turn (0.9999 -> 1.0234 in six) and lowered K to 0.7476. Deployment of savings is itself a basket-price move at that depth. |
| 4 | B, C | A 5% coherent sell-off in B: BVIB 1.1021, K 0.7500 -> 0.7384 -> 0.7369. The household's exit preview was /not/ refused: the leveraged basket's collateral still covered its principal. The same 5% in C also left every redemption open. |
| 5 | B | The machine's response then sold about 7.2k BUCK-worth a day and BVIB decayed 1.1021 -> 1.0746 -> 1.0532 -> 1.0323 -> 1.0118 in four days; K recovered to 0.7436. The household's cost/fair went 0.908, 0.931, 0.950, 0.969, 0.989: on the fourth day its adaptive target was a sixth of the first day's, on the fifth there was no plan. |
| 6 | A | Retiring 50 BUCK of principal with a 10-BUCK returnable premium deposit took two reductions: 49.87 from the proceeds, then 0.13 from the returned deposit. A purchase-funded retirement of the same target took one. |
| 7 | A, C | Python: the seventeen surplus cases run in 13 s, the 258-test supported set in 82 s. JavaScript under Tevm: the surplus fixture takes about nineteen minutes; one debtor's refinance, save and wallet prepayment alone take 299 s, a saving case 8 to 20 s and a retirement 24 to 48 s. |
| 8 | A, C | pyrevm refuses an =eth_call= whose sender is a contract (EIP-3607); Tevm accepts one (probed directly with a contract sender). Every simulated household is a proxy contract. |

**** Recommendations

1. /Compose one owner dispatcher for the act loop./ Refinancing and surplus
   share one receipt cursor, so a household runs at most one flow at a time.
   Keep the legacy loop's order and recover once: calendar; a known refused
   settlement (=settlement_due=); a known refused deposit (=surplus_due=);
   a due asset exit, which preempts the rest every day until it clears; on
   a decision day a fresh refinance tranche, saver issuance or investor
   choice; then surplus; then working money. Today
   =HouseholdRefinance.start= does not test =surplus_due=, so a refinance
   started first would replace the cursor and silently drop a deposit retry
   (the purchased cash stays owned, but the intention and its =acquired=
   amount are lost). Put the retry before new credit, as cash-only
   resumption already is, and add that interleave to the shared vectors.
2. /Let known progress finish the same day./ A funded retirement needs one
   to three dispatcher turns (the returned premium funds the last
   reduction), and every recovery consumes a turn. With one call a day a
   retirement straddles days while its protected reserve keeps the household
   out of other decisions. Give the act loop a small bounded number of turns
   a day for /known/ states only; unknown outcomes must still stop.
3. /Step through every payday./ A calendar refuses to skip one. Any driver
   that jumps days (the savings sandbox step control, population arrivals, a
   resumed world) must visit each household at least once a pay period.
4. /Use the read-only owner dispatch for every preview./ Asset exits and
   saver credit investment will want to simulate as the owner. On pyrevm that
   must be =d.net_simulate=; a direct call fails before the contract runs.
   Tevm would accept the direct call, but the JavaScript world passes the
   same proxy twin so both simulators run one path. Promote the adapter
   into the credit and market SDK objects when the first of those needs it,
   rather than adding a second mechanism. The browser wallet sandbox uses
   EOAs and keeps the direct default, which no test here exercises yet.
5. /Expect household discounted retirement to be brief and second-order./
   Observation 5: the machine repaired a 10% basket-relative discount in
   about five days, and the household signal decays with it. Default
   households decide every 5 to 20 days (keyed), with thresholds of 0.5% to
   3%. Most will not be looking during the window. In the five-year metrics
   report the /opportunity/ (household-days beyond each threshold) beside
   the response (=hhPaidDown=, funded and confirmed retirement), and run one
   comparison with a daily surplus cadence before concluding anything about
   this channel from a small paydown total.
6. /A dollar-pool shock is not a household discount./ The savings sandbox's
   shock actor leans on BUCK/USDC only. Households respond after arbitrage
   carries the dislocation into the TOKEN pools and the TWAP passes
   (observations 1 and 2). Keep wheel arbitrage on for shock
   comparisons, measure route consistency and basket-relative value
   separately as already planned, and add a broad sell-off (the same share
   of every BUCK pool) to the shock set so the household channel can be
   tested directly. In the sandbox show the dollar price and the
   cost/fair ratio side by side; otherwise a stress button that moves only
   one of them looks like a bug.
7. /Size the pools to the population's flows./ Each saving is capped at
   0.5% (at =max_impact_bp= 100) of the dollar pool's reserve, about a 1%
   price move per purchase by constant-product arithmetic; households decide
   independently, so their purchases add. Observation 3 is the TOKEN-pool
   analogue. Before the campaigns, compare expected daily always-invest
   dollars and wheel deployment against =deploy.target_buck_lp_m= /
   =target_buck_m= and against a day of arbitrage and response capacity, and
   report the dollar-route premium and BVIB separately. A world whose pools
   are thin against its savers will show a persistent route premium that is
   an artifact of sizing.
8. /Watch new credit, not only shortfall, when K falls./ Observation 2: K
   moves at once and keeps moving while a discount stands. Activated
   capacity is K times coverage, so staged refinance programmes stall
   before any facility is short. Chart issuance room and declined
   =insufficient-capacity= assessments through each shock. Do not change the
   controller's sign or gains on this evidence; it is one small fixture.
9. /Find where the exit door closes before the shock campaigns./ In
   observation 4 redemptions stayed open at K 0.737. The K (or excursion) at
   which the leveraged basket's own shortfall makes =_exitSnapshot= refuse
   was /not/ measured, and no fixture here produced a real K refusal of an
   exit; only an injected one. A household with a closed door and no spare
   dollars can neither retire nor leave. Add one directed contract-level
   case (lower K until =CreditShortfallIncreased=, then let the wheel's
   bounded retirement reopen it) and the matching household wait.
10. /Decide receipt-first versus cash-first by measurement./ A retire plan
    redeems a receipt before spending dollars. Redemption draws the basket's
    cash (or makes it unwind liquidity) and puts no bid in the dollar pool;
    buying does. Both reduce supply. Make the order a profile setting and
    include it in the policy grid.
11. /Unknown outcomes are a sandbox matter./ In-process runs lose no
    replies, so five-year pyrevm campaigns will not exercise them. The
    savings server and browser will. Surface =status().retirement= and the
    pending hash, and give the operator a reconcile-from-journal action;
    otherwise one lost reply freezes a household, its wages included, for
    the rest of the session.
12. /Assess once a day./ A debtor's surplus assessment runs the whole
    refinance assessor for its funding floor (78 options and 75 issuance
    quotes in the interleave fixture), and the refinancing component then
    runs it again. That is negligible on pyrevm and dominant under Tevm
    (observation 7). Share one same-block assessment between the two
    components, and pin and batch its reads, before a JavaScript population
    world is attempted. The Python campaign remains the five-year vehicle.
13. /Carry the new state into saved worlds when they arrive./ The lasting
    =_net_credit_reserve=, the surplus cursor (=kind=, =acquired=, quoted
    terms, =preserved=) and the surplus cadence days are not yet in any
    import format. Uninterrupted runs do not need them.
14. /Reconcile the reports with the new records./ The report's
    surplus-policy chart reads household totals. The history's distinction
    between a funded target and confirmed reductions, and
    =surplus_protection=, is in the JSON but not charted. The fixtures hold
    =unwound= equal to the sum of confirmed principal; assert the same over
    the first short complete world, and chart funded against confirmed
    there rather than after a five-year run.
15. /Run whole targets at each checkpoint./ A focused subset hid a failing
    supported test for five checkpoints. Before a campaign run
    =make nix-venv-economy-accounting-test=, =make nix-venv-net-demurrage-smoke=
    and =make nix-test= at the exact revision and record the counts.
16. /The sandbox actors are unverified on canonical money./ =ShockAgent=
    still draws with the numeric =mint= overload and swaps through its own
    callback. Both remain in canonical Buck, but neither was exercised here.
    Qualify it with the first savings-sandbox world.

** Working-money milestone: the household wallet (complete)

Continued from the surplus checkpoint =44ea3f6c=. No Solidity, demurrage
kernel, K rule or Jubilee allowance changed. Normal act remains gated.

- [X] Size the working balance with the existing =money_target= from the
  same basket-relative cost/fair ratio as saving, taking the discount in
  parts per billion so Python and JavaScript size the same raw target
  (=net_wallet_target= / =netWalletTarget=, shared vectors).
- [X] Choose one capped bite toward that target from a pinned market
  (=net_wallet_adjustment= / =netWalletAdjustment=): buy below 95%, sell the
  excess above 110%, never the protected dollars, never past half the impact
  allowance, never under one whole unit. Ten shared vectors.
- [X] Execute on the existing receipt cursor as =kind=wallet=. A purchase is
  paid by the main account and delivered by the guard straight to the
  wallet's own Ordinary account; a sale is made from the wallet's custody
  with the dollars delivered home. Neither touches credit or the main BUCK.
- [X] Tend once after each payday on the existing owner dispatcher
  (=advance_net_wallet= / =advanceNetWallet=), after recovery and the
  calendar; a refused or declined attempt waits for the next payday.
- [X] Share the debtor's credit dollar front between the day's refinance
  assessment and later same-block saving and working-money choices
  (=credit_cash_floor= / =creditCashFloor=), the first half of
  recommendation 12 above.
- [X] Actual Python and JavaScript lifecycle (first purchase, ramp and
  decay top-up, capped trim) and lost, refused and unknown outcomes.

*** Working-money checkpoint

- *What the wallet is*: ordinary money in a second bound Ordinary account,
  created with its trading approvals on first use and before any flow. Its
  balance carries demurrage. The legacy =_tend_wallet= remains for legacy
  accounting only; it bought from all cash, where the canonical component
  spends only dollars above the buffer and credit front.
- *Machine*: =WalletPlan=, =assess_wallet= / =assessWallet=,
  =start_wallet= / =startWallet= and one =buy= or =sell= phase. Lost replies
  are authenticated from the saved hash (a purchase by buyer, recipient and
  pool; a sale by seller, pool and dollar recipient) and applied once.
  Completion reports =outcome=wallet-buy= or =wallet-sell=; a hold is a
  declined =kind=wallet= observation with its market record.
  =status()= gains =next_wallet_day=; the agent accumulates =wallet_bought=
  and =wallet_sold=.
- *Evidence at this checkpoint* (final sources; counts, not log paths):
  - Python, the same sixteen supported files: *263 passed*, no skips, 82 s
    (five more than the surplus checkpoint: the wallet policy vectors, the
    lifecycle and three fault cases).
  - Fast JavaScript, =make nix-core-equilibrium-policy-test=: *79 passed*.
  - Actual JavaScript wallet fixture
    (=test/net-household-wallet.tevm.test.js=, one Tevm world): *11 Node
    checks passed* (ten children and the parent), no skips, three and a
    half minutes.
  - Full actual JavaScript equilibrium acceptance, now six files run
    together: *99 passed*, no skips, about thirty minutes.
  - =make nix-venv-net-demurrage-smoke=: *35 Python and 20 JavaScript
    passed*; the browser equilibrium bundle rebuilds.
- *Commands*:
  #+begin_src sh
  make nix-venv-test-python PYTEST_FILES=alberta_buck/test/test_net_household_setup.py PYTEST_ARGS='-k wallet -q'
  make nix-core-equilibrium-bootstrap-test CORE_EQUILIBRIUM_TEST_FILES=test/net-household-wallet.tevm.test.js
  #+end_src

*** Observations and recommendations for the five-year runs and sandboxes (working money)

Additions to the list above; the same caution applies. Fixture A is the
Python =household_world=; the household spends 60k a year, holds one month of
it, and starts at half adoption.

**** Measured in this phase

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 9 | A | First payday: a 2,705.34-BUCK target cost 2,718.51 dollars, 0.49% over one for one in fee and impact on a 1M pool, and landed on target to the unit. |
| 10 | A | Thirty days later the untouched balance was 2,700.90 (the 2% a year), the ramped target 2,910.68, and the top-up 211.77 dollars. A steady target still buys about 0.16% of itself a month. |
| 11 | A | A 19,757-BUCK excess sold 4,985 BUCK in its one bite, the impact cap of 0.5% of the pool; shedding it takes four paydays. |
| 12 | policy | =money_gain= 200 makes the target very sensitive: a 0.1% basket-relative discount adds a fifth, 0.5% doubles it, and 3.5% reaches the eightfold cap. The 5% sell-off of observation 4 would ask each household for eight times its balance at its next payday. |
| 13 | C | Under Tevm a payday with its wallet trade takes 13 to 19 s for a mortgage-free saver (wages, two bills, then the trade; the first also creates and approves the wallet account). The whole wallet fixture runs in three and a half minutes. Tevm's clock also moves with every transaction, so a balance read before a payday is a few raw units older by the time it is assessed; pyrevm's does not. |

**** Recommendations

17. /Put the wallet where the legacy loop had it./ Working money was tended
    inside the monthly flow, before issuance and saving. Keep that in the
    act loop: calendar, known remainders, a due exit, then on a payday the
    wallet, then the decision-day choices. It already declines while a
    settlement or deposit retry is due.
18. /Paydays were synchronized/ (resolved below). Every household's payday
    was its arrival day plus thirty, so an opening cohort tended its wallets
    on the same days for five years. Each bite is capped against the reserves
    it sees, but the bites add within the day (observation 9: about a quarter
    of a percent of a 1M pool's dollars per household), and with the
    eightfold discount response of observation 12 that was the largest
    single-day BUCK demand in the model. The owner chose on 2026-11-12 to
    give each household its own fixed day of the month; see the payday
    milestone. Runs made before and after that change are not comparable day
    for day.
19. /Expect asymmetric unwinding./ A discount fills wallets quickly (each
    buys toward eight times its balance), and parity afterwards leaves them
    above 110% of target, selling one capped bite a payday (observation 11).
    A shock is followed by months of small scheduled BUCK sales from
    wallets. Chart wallet balances against target, and =wallet_bought= and
    =wallet_sold= by day, so that tail is not read as a second shock.
20. /Wallet demand is the model's standing BUCK demand./ Unlike savings it
    is not deposited: it sits in Ordinary accounts and pays demurrage to
    the fund. Report total wallet BUCK beside basket deposits and issued
    principal in the supply reconciliation, and include wallets when
    asking who holds the float (the earlier hoarding artifact was found
    that way).
21. /A lost wallet reply stops the household, as any other./ The sandbox
    remarks under recommendation 11 apply unchanged; the wallet's own
    account adds one more address the operator may need to see.

** Payday milestone: each household's own day of the month (complete)

The owner's instruction of 2026-11-12, answering recommendation 18: fix each
household's payday on a fixed random day of the month.

- [X] =pay_day=, a household setting: the day of the 30-day month, counted
  from the simulation's day 0. Its default is the range 0 to 30, so the
  existing keyed profile draws a whole day 0 to 29 per member; a whole number
  pins it. The draw is keyed by member and setting name and moves no other
  parameter.
- [X] =first_payday(arrive_day, pay_day)= / =firstPayday=: the first such day
  strictly after arrival, 1 to 30 days later; every later payday is 30 days
  on. Used by the agent, the canonical calendar's bind, the legacy monthly
  loop and the wallet's cadence, in both languages.
- [X] Fixtures pin their payday to the arrival's day of the month, which
  reproduces the old "thirty days after arrival" chronology exactly: in the
  shared JSON settings where every household arrives on day 0, and in code
  where a fixture varies the arrival day.
- [X] Shared vectors for the rule, a keyed-spread check over 200 members, and
  an actual pair in each wallet fixture: a household paid on the 7th is paid
  and tends its wallet on day 7 while its neighbour waits for day 30.

*** Payday checkpoint

- *Evidence*: the supported Python set, 264 passed (sixteen files, no skips, 81 s on
  pyrevm); fast JavaScript policy units, 80 passed; the actual JavaScript
  equilibrium acceptance, 100 passed across six Tevm fixture files (no skips,
  about 30 min); the repayment-engine smoke, 35 Python and 20 JavaScript; the
  browser bundle rebuilds.

*** Observations and recommendations for the five-year runs and sandboxes (paydays)

**** Measured in this phase

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 14 | policy | Over 200 keyed members every one of the 30 days is somebody's payday; the quietest day has 2 of them and the busiest 12. A cohort's wallet and bill flows now spread over the month instead of landing on one day. |
| 15 | arithmetic | The first period is 1 to 30 days, not always 30. Over 1,825 days a household has 60 or 61 paydays depending on its day, so total wages and instalments differ by up to one in sixty between households. Each household's counterfactual book shares its paydays. |

**** Recommendations

22. /Compare like with like./ A world run with keyed paydays is not
    day-for-day comparable with an earlier run, and aggregate income differs
    slightly by cohort composition (observation 15). Record =pay_day= with
    each household in the vectors (it is in the profile already) and do not
    mix old and new baselines in one comparison.
23. /Daily stepping is now essential./ With paydays on every day of the
    month, any driver that advances more than a day at a time skips
    somebody's payday, which the calendar refuses. The runner's =day_step=1=
    and the sandbox's step control must visit every day.
24. /Recheck the single-day peak./ The largest same-day demand is now
    about a seventeenth of the cohort's (observation 14), not all of it. Recommendation 7's sizing
    comparison should use the busiest payday in the actual roster, and the
    decision-day cadence (keyed phases over 5 to 20 days) is the next
    largest source of coincidence.
25. /Pin paydays in every new fixture./ A fixture that leaves =pay_day=
    unset gets a keyed day and will not be paid on day 30. Pin it, in the
    shared JSON when the household arrives on day 0, in code otherwise.

** Release milestone: =burn(0)= releases coverage no principal needs (complete)

The owner's decisions of 2026-11-12 (Start here, decisions table). This is the
only Solidity change since the canonical foundation was qualified.

- [X] =BuckRenewalOps.planRelease(cash, state, allowances, d, owner, ids, k)=
  (view): for each selected facility in order, the largest coverage whose
  release leaves its principal fully backed at =k=. No principal: all of it.
  With principal: =active - keep=, where =keep= is the least activated face
  with =floor(floor(mark * keep / face) * k / 1e18) >= principal=, computed as
  two ceilings (=ceil(principal * 1e18 / k)=, then =ceil(value * face /
  mark)=); a facility at or beyond its limit releases nothing. The refund is
  =floor(premium * released / active)=. A facility whose refund its original
  pool cannot pay now is skipped whole (pool unbound or not a verified
  Carrying account; allowance to the token; nominal balance; for a sponsored
  owner, the sponsor's funds for the pool's carried fee). Shared pools, the
  sponsor and a Carrying owner's own pool are carried virtually through the
  batch, as =planBurn= does.
- [X] =BuckRenewalOps.release(...)=: verify the caller, settle its cash,
  compute K afresh, plan, then per facility refund through the existing
  =refundPremium=, reduce the recorded deposit, =deactivateFromBuck=, clear
  the recorded pool when no principal remains, re-check backing, emit
  =FacilityReleased=. Final checks: the caller's balance did not fall and K
  did not move. It does not touch the fund: no valuation refresh, no reserve
  checkpoint, no relief budget.
- [X] =Buck.burn(0)= / =burn(0, ids)= dispatch to =release=; =burn(N > 0)= is
  byte-for-byte the old path. =quoteBurn(0, ids)= returns the plan's totals at
  the last computed K. =FacilityReleased(owner, tokenId, coverage,
  premiumRefund)= is declared on =Buck= for its ABI.
- [X] SDK, both languages: =quote_release= / =quoteRelease= (as the owner,
  through the same read-only owner dispatch the basket preview uses),
  =release= (submit and authenticate), =release_from_receipt= /
  =releaseFromReceipt= (every event names this owner and one selected NFT,
  once, in the submitted order; nothing released is a valid success), and
  =ReleasePending= carrying the hash of an unauthenticated outcome.
  =NetCreditOps= / =netCreditOps= take the optional =simulate= adapter.
- [X] Tests: =test/BuckRelease.t.sol= (11: nothing drawn; deposit-only draw
  and the later burn; excess after K rises; a shortfall that must not move;
  explicit selection, duplicate and foreign; a pool that cannot refund by
  balance and by allowance, with the rest released; one pool shared across
  the batch in order; unverified and Carrying owners; independence from the
  fund valuation; a sponsored owner refunded in full or skipped; and a fuzz
  over face, depreciation, both K values and the draw that checks the kept
  coverage is the least that backs the principal). One Python and one
  JavaScript SDK test against real contracts, including a lost reply
  recovered from its receipt.

*** Release checkpoint

- *Evidence*: =test/BuckRelease.t.sol= 11 passed (the fuzz case 256
  runs); =make nix-test= 1,547 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed; =make
  nix-contracts-dist-check= passes; =make nix-sim-build= rebuilds. The
  supported Python set 265 passed (sixteen files, with the SDK release
  test); the repayment-engine smoke 35 Python and 21 JavaScript (with the
  JavaScript SDK release test); fast JavaScript 80; the actual JavaScript
  equilibrium acceptance 100 passed across six Tevm files, no skips, 31
  min; the browser bundle rebuilds.

*** Observations and recommendations for the five-year runs and sandboxes (release)

**** Measured in this phase

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 16 | build | Deployed sizes after the change: =Buck= 21,795 bytes (was 21,160), =BuckRenewalOps= 11,811 (was 6,432), =BuckFacilities= 23,943, unchanged and 633 bytes under the 24,576 limit. Nothing more fits in =BuckFacilities=. |
| 17 | Foundry | After a release, burning the owner's whole balance leaves one raw unit of principal (and up to two of coverage, one of deposit): a numeric burn settles the /least/ principal that lowers the balance by its amount, and the proportional refund rounds down. Deposit 100, release, then =burn(90)= retires 99.999999. |
| 18 | Foundry | A released facility sits exactly at its K limit: a second release finds nothing, and any fall in K afterwards is a shortfall (which blocks new issuance but is never penalized). Issued at K = 0.5 to its limit, then K = 0.8: 375 of 1,000 coverage and 37.5 of the 100 deposit come back; 625 stays. |
| 19 | Foundry, SDK | With one pool holding enough for one refund only, the first facility in the submitted order is released and the second is skipped; the transaction succeeds. The quote reported the same. |
| 20 | JavaScript SDK | Under Tevm the claim-age total keeps growing by whole seconds on the unchanged principal while the test runs; under pyrevm it is exactly equal before and after. Release itself adds or removes none. |

**** Recommendations

26. /An exit closes with the selected-facility engine, not a numeric burn./
    Retiring a named facility's whole principal releases all its coverage and
    refunds all its deposit in the same transaction. A numeric
    =burn(balance)= leaves raw-unit dust (observation 17), which would keep an
    exiting household's NFT open forever. Use =burn(0)= only afterwards, for
    coverage that no principal backs.
27. /Release only when leaving./ A household that keeps its asset pays the
    external insurer for every unactivated share
    (=external_premium_due=), and loses its activated room, so releasing
    excess coverage mid-life raises its bills and its next issue's premium.
    The economy's only caller should be the asset exit. If a later policy
    wants a mid-life release (for example to recover a deposit when K has
    risen far), score it against that external premium first.
28. /A release that releases nothing is a success: read back./ A pool that
    cannot refund, or a sponsor that cannot cover the pool's carried fee,
    skips the facility silently (observation 19). The exit stage must check
    the facilities' activated coverage afterwards, report
    =release-waiting= with the pool's state, and try again on a later day; it
    must not treat the successful transaction as a completed release.
29. /The quote is at the last computed K./ Execution computes K afresh, and
    the PID controller can move K when time has passed, so a quote and its
    execution can differ. Authenticate the events (the SDK does) and book
    those amounts; never book the quote.
30. /Report it./ Add released coverage and refunded deposit to the household
    exit records and to the campaign's supply reconciliation: a refund moves
    BUCK from an insurer pool to a household without changing supply or
    principal, so "pool balance equals the sum of recorded deposits" must be
    checked after releases as well as after reductions.
31. /No room left beside =burn=./ Any further facility logic belongs in
    =BuckRenewalOps= or a new linked library (which then must be added to the
    Makefile's artifact list, =buck_core/net_world.py=, =core/js/src/net-world.js=
    and =scripts/contracts-dist.py=).

** Credit-investment milestone: a mortgage-free household issues, keeps and deposits (Python complete; JavaScript ported in the opened-loop milestone)

Step 2 of Start here. A saver, or a debtor whose mortgage is paid, issues
against its insured asset and keeps the BUCK, depositing them in the basket
under its own account.

- [X] =assess_investment= / =assessInvestment=: one block; the amount wanted
  is =investment_want= (the =premium= or =carry= basis); each facility's
  bounded =issue_coverage_menu= at the five size fractions; the lasting credit
  reserve kept; a reserve shortfall funded by a guarded purchase; the closing
  fee fronted in dollars above the buffer; each candidate scored by
  =investment_quoted= with the dollars its new BUCK would fetch at that
  block's dollar pool. Terms are built with =spendable = 0=, so wallet money
  is never counted as capacity.
- [X] /A bite of a line./ One decision's amount is bounded by the dollar
  pool (half of =max_impact_bp= of its BUCK reserve), so a first bite can never
  repay the one-time closing fee alone. As refinancing does, the assessor then
  quotes the whole line now (=investment_programme=: the remaining carry line
  at today's K; for a premium only the bite itself, because a premium is a
  passing market state), requires that programme to pass with the full fee and
  to be affordable from present dollars, and scores the bite with the fee
  allocated by dollars (=staged_investment= / =stagedInvestment=). The real
  payment is still the whole fee, once.
- [X] Flow =kind=invest= on the same cursor: =closing= (the fee, paid to the
  bank first; skipped once =refinance_closed=), =fund= (reserve purchase),
  =issue=, =deposit= (at most the newly issued spendable, above the reserve).
  A refused deposit keeps the issued BUCK owned and is =surplus_due=: it is
  retried alone before any new credit. The plan's reserve becomes the
  household's lasting credit front, as in refinancing.
- [X] =advance_net_investment= / =advanceNetInvestment=: the owner dispatcher,
  on the keyed decision cadence, at most one assessment a day; it does
  nothing while a mortgage remains (refinancing owns a debtor's decisions).
- [X] Shared fixture =test/fixtures/household-invest.json=: the lifecycle
  (closing once, then issue and deposit each day, the same receipt topped up),
  the premium trigger (buying the same share of every TOKEN pool makes the
  BUCK dear against the basket once its TWAP passes), fourteen fault cases
  (lost, unknown and refused for each of the four phases, and an unavailable
  read before the issue and before the deposit), and pure vectors for the
  programme score.

*** Credit-investment checkpoint

- *Evidence*: =alberta_buck/test/test_net_household_invest.py= 16
  passed on pyrevm (lifecycle, premium trigger, fourteen fault cases); the
  pure programme and staged-score tests pass in Python; the whole supported
  Python set 294 passed with one strict expected failure (the JavaScript
  conformance). No JavaScript, so not qualified.

*** Observations and recommendations for the five-year runs and sandboxes (credit investment)

**** Measured in this phase

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 21 | Python lifecycle | The first bite of a 224,775 BUCK carry line (30% of a 1M home's K = 0.75 limit) is 4,995 BUCK: 0.5% of the 1M dollar pool, although nothing is sold there. The line is 45 bites. Scored alone the bite is worth -1,698 dollars against its 2,000 closing fee (carry about 100 a year, three-year horizon); scored as a bite of the line it carries 44.44 of the fee and is worth +227. |
| 22 | Python lifecycle | One bite: 2,000 USDC closing; 178.83 USDC buys the 178.71 BUCK funding reserve (half of the premium at parity); issue 5,351.79 principal = 4,995 spendable + 356.79 premium deposit (5% of 7,135.71 coverage); deposit 4,989.83 (the 0.1% sizing cushion stays). Later bites send only the issue and the deposit and top up the same receipt. |
| 23 | Python | One investment assessment reads 75 actual candidates in 0.29 s on pyrevm, 90 in 0.43 s when the programme is quoted too. A household with nothing wanted returns =hold= before quoting. |
| 24 | Python premium | With no carry line a saver holds at BVIB = 1.00. After the seeding helper buys BUCK with a twentieth of every TOKEN pool's TOKEN and a day passes, BVIB is more than 5% lower, the basis is =premium=, and the score's execution term is positive: kept, the new BUCK are valued at the basket; sold they would fetch more. |

**** Recommendations

32. /Pool depth decides whether savers ever join on a premium./ A premium's
    programme is the bite itself, so the bite alone must repay the closing
    fee: roughly =bite * (premium + theta * net carry rate) > fee=. In the
    1M-BUCK fixture pool a 5,000 bite never can; a household needs a pool
    about twenty times its bite for a 1 to 2% premium to pay a 2,000 fee.
    Size the dollar pool to the population (the economy file already does)
    and report, per run, how many savers joined and on which basis. If none
    join on a premium, the dear-BUCK supply response is coming from debtors
    alone.
33. /A carry line is 45 decisions long./ At one bite per decision and a
    keyed cadence of 5 to 20 days, filling a line takes 225 to 900 days.
    A five-year run sees it fill; a 30-day smoke sees two or three bites.
    When comparing short and long runs, compare bites issued, not lines
    filled.
34. /Credit investment is supply and reserve demand together./ Each bite
    raises supply by its principal (the spendable and the premium deposit)
    and sends the spendable to the basket, which repays its own lien or
    deploys; the first bite also buys its funding reserve in the dollar pool.
    Add issued-to-invest principal, premium deposits and reserve purchases to
    the campaign's supply reconciliation, beside the wallet and the basket.
35. /The small remainder accumulates./ About 0.1% of each bite stays in the
    main account above the reserve (5 BUCK a bite here) and pays demurrage.
    It is swept by a later deposit only when it exceeds a deposit's grain.
    Report main-account idle BUCK for savers; if it grows, let the deposit
    take all owned BUCK above the reserve rather than only the new issue.
36. /A refused deposit is retried before new credit./ The dispatcher resumes
    it on the next day. In the unified loop (step 5) keep that order: a
    household must never issue again while earlier issued BUCK wait undeposited.
37. /The saver's fee is paid first, and that is now what the code does./ The
    open question in the decisions table is settled in the implementation:
    =closing= precedes =fund= and =issue=. A refusal after it leaves the fee
    paid and =refinance_closed= set, so the next plan carries no fee.

** Exit, investor and dispatcher milestone (Python first; JavaScript ported in the opened-loop milestone)

Steps 3 to 5 of the earlier list, in Python. All three are owner-dispatcher
components on the same receipt cursor; nothing here changes Solidity.

- [X] /Asset sale on the calendar./ =schedule_sale= queues a =sale= period on
  the exit day: the insured face value is credited from an authenticated
  dollar mint (like wages, but not counted as income, and it sets =_sold=,
  ending the external premium), then arrears and the /whole/ mortgage are
  paid in the same period. =schedule_settlement= queues a =settle= period
  (from =living=) when dollars arrive later. The counterfactual sells the same
  asset the same day and pays its mortgage.
- [X] /=advance_net_exit=, one step a turn./ After recovery it chooses from
  what the chain and books show now, in order: sell the asset; settle; sell
  the working balance (a capped bite); redeem each basket receipt (whole, or
  a half, quarter or eighth the basket can pay in BUCK, previewed as the
  owner); retire the whole lien with owned BUCK through the selected-facility
  engine (reserve one raw unit); buy the rest back (a surplus =retire= plan, a
  capped bite); =burn(0)= any coverage still activated (=ExitPlan("release")=);
  sell owned BUCK a capped bite at a time; sell TOKEN an in-kind exit paid;
  then mark the household =exited=. One /market/ bite a day (=pace=, not a
  retry); a step that cannot run today (closed exit door, proved refusal, no
  dollars) =wait=s, is recorded once and counted in =exit_retries=; no
  dollars for the lien sets =exit_short= (=hhDivestShort=) and waits for
  wages. A known refused remainder is =abandon=ed at the start, and the
  household protects no dollar buffer while leaving. Other dispatchers
  return False once the exit day has come.
- [X] /Investor./ =advance_net_investor= on the keyed cadence:
  =net_investor_action= (integer: the BUCK is dear when (1 - fee) > BVIB x
  (1 + premium), the dollar pool's price cancelling as for every household)
  buys BUCK with spare dollars and /holds/ them (=SurplusPlan("hold")=), or
  deposits what is held (=SurplusPlan("convert")=, deposit only). Its
  withdrawal at =hold_years= is the same exit without asset or lien.
- [X] /Dispatcher./ =_act_net= written (see step B); not wired.
- [X] Shared fixture =test/fixtures/household-exit.json=; Python tests
  =alberta_buck/test/test_net_household_exit.py= (11): a saver with a carry
  line, wallet and receipt; a debtor after one refinance tranche; an investor
  that buys, converts when dear, and withdraws; a backing-only facility that
  only =burn(0)= can release; and seven fault cases (lost, unknown and refused
  redemption, a closed exit door, lost and unknown sale, and a cash shortfall
  that resumes when dollars and the receipt return).

  =test_net_household_invest.py= 16 passed on pyrevm; the whole supported
  Python set (eighteen files) 294 passed with one strict expected failure (the
  JavaScript conformance of step A). No JavaScript, so not qualified.

*** Observations and recommendations for the five-year runs and sandboxes (exits)

**** Measured in this phase

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 25 | Python saver exit | After two carry bites (lien 15,973.75 BUCK, receipt basis 29,728.68): exit day, the 1,000,000 USDC sale; the receipt redeemed whole for 29,647.39 BUCK, paid in BUCK; one reduction retired the whole 15,973.75 and refunded the whole 1,064.91 deposit, so =burn(0)= had nothing left; the owned BUCK sold in four daily bites (4,925, 4,950, 4,974, 91) for 15,160 USDC. Four days in all. |
| 26 | Python | Before the one-bite-a-day rule the same exit sold three bites within one day, each into a pool the previous bite had moved. |
| 27 | Python release | A facility activated with no premium and nothing drawn (backing only) leaves the coverage behind after the sale; =burn(0)= releases all 250,000 of it and refunds nothing. This is the only exit that needs the release. |
| 28 | Python investor | It buys about 0.5% of the dollar pool's depth per decision and holds it; after the seeding helper buys BUCK with a twentieth of every TOKEN pool, the next decision deposits all of it; at the horizon it redeems and sells. Supply never changes: an investor has no lien. |

**** Recommendations

38. /Exit selling is BUCK supply into the dollar pool./ Each exit sells a
    household's owned BUCK (its redeemed savings above the lien, its wallet)
    a capped bite a day. At =divest_rate = .1= a 200-household population
    starts an exit about every 18 days; a recession cell that raises it would
    put many sellers in one pool. Chart exit sales per day against the pool's
    depth and against BUCK/USDC.
39. /Full retirement releases its own coverage./ Retiring a facility's whole
    principal returns all its coverage and deposit in the same transaction,
    so =burn(0)= at exit is needed only for backing-only facilities or
    coverage left after a partial retirement. Count both in the report; a
    nonzero release on an ordinary facility means a retirement stopped short.
40. /A closed exit door waits, it does not force./ When the basket's own K
    gate refuses the redemption preview, the household waits, counted once a
    day. Report households waiting and their days, per cell. Measure where
    the door closes (lower K until =_exitSnapshot= reverts): no fixture has
    produced a real refusal yet.
41. /Insolvency is visible, not completed./ =exit_short= households keep
    their lien and wait for wages, which continue until they leave. Report
    them separately; never count them as exited.
42. /The sale price is the insured face value./ There is no house-price
    model; a recession cell that should cut house prices needs one, applied
    to actual and counterfactual alike.
43. /Exit events are the reconciliation record./ The final =complete= event
    carries =withdrawn_usdc=, =released_coverage=, =premium_refund_nominal= and
    =retired_principal=; the campaign's books check should sum them over the
    exited households against supply and pool balances.


** Investor tolerance, JavaScript port and the opened act loop (complete; the centre's falling K is open)

The owner's answers of 2026-11-13 and steps A and B.

- [X] /Investor tolerance./ =bvib_tolerance=, a keyed draw per investor,
  normal about 1% with sd 0.5%, held within 0..2%. =net_investor_action(bvib,
  tolerance)=: =invest= while BVIB <= 1 + tolerance, otherwise =wait=.
  =assess_investor=: investing, deposit BUCK held (=convert=) or buy and
  deposit (=save=); waiting, buy BUCK and hold them (=hold=). Both languages.
- [X] /Keyed normal draws./ =normal(seed, member, key, mean, sd, lo, hi)=:
  the sum of twelve keyed uniforms less six (Irwin-Hall), clamped to the
  bounds. Pure addition in the same order, so Python and JavaScript produce
  the same double (tested over 400 members). A setting given as
  ={mean, sd, lo, hi}= is drawn this way; a scalar still pins it.
- [X] /Step A, JavaScript port./ Calendar =sale= / =settle=;
  =assessInvestment=, =assessInvestor=; on =HouseholdRefinance=:
  =startInvestment=, =startExit=, =abandon=, the shared =prepareIssue= /
  =prepareDeposit= / =bookDeposit=, the =hold= and =convert= surplus
  actions; on the agent: =advanceNetInvestment=, =advanceNetInvestor=,
  =advanceNetExit= with its =exitStep=; =stagedInvestment=,
  =investmentProgramme=, =netInvestorAction= in the policy module. Tests:
  =core/js/test/net-household-invest.tevm.test.js= and
  =net-household-exit.tevm.test.js= on the shared fixtures; seven fault
  cases are Python-only (="js": false=) to bound the Tevm run, the rest
  cover every phase and outcome kind.
- [X] /Step B, the act loop is open./ =act= calls =_act_net= / =actNet= on
  canonical accounting in both languages. Recovery comes first, as before (a
  recovered or uncertain outcome consumes the turn); then the calendar binds
  or accrues; then the components in the documented order. The ten fixture
  failures were fixed in the tests, not by loosening the agent: bare-agent
  fixtures recover before any machine is touched; the driver stub gained the
  display cache (=proxy.bump=) a real driver builds (the "not diagnosed"
  =AttributeError=); the two gate tests now assert the opened behaviour. In
  JavaScript =act= also recovers first and returns before reading or
  recording anything; a first version called =record= after a recovery and
  failed three stub-world smoke tests.
- [X] /Reading a world./ =python -m alberta_buck.sim.digest=: K, BVIB,
  price, the supply change split by source, who holds the BUCK at the start
  and the end (the BUCK/USDC pool, the TOKEN/BUCK pools, the basket,
  everyone else), each role's outcome and the refusal kinds, from a vector
  alone.
- [X] /First complete worlds./ =make nix-venv-economy-smoke ECONOMY_SMOKE_DAYS=30= completes on
  canonical accounting (observations 29 to 35); then 90 and 365 days, and two
  one-year variants (observations 36 to 45).
- [X] /The JavaScript CLIs load the canonical world./ =eqsim.mjs=, =farmsim.mjs=
  and =tevm-membench.mjs= used the packaged-artifact loader, which has no
  =Permit2=; they now use =loadAnyArtifact= (the vendored builds too), the
  loader the tests' bundle is built from. A 30-day =make nix-core-eqsim= (one
  debtor, one basket saver, Tevm in Node) now builds its world and runs on
  the opened loop; it reached day 25 in 35 minutes, about 80 seconds a
  simulated day, mostly the work wheel's ticks. A JavaScript population
  world is impractical on Tevm; an in-browser world must stay small.

Both SDKs now quote the minimum as the basket prices it (=quote_deposit_shares= / =quoteDepositShares=: =equity() - gross() + grossAt(HIGH)=, less the charge). Zero-tolerance year: of +6.61M, TOKEN/BUCK pools +4.32M (65%), basket +0.52M, everyone else +2.19M (the investors' 2.1M held as money). The basket issues against its equity and buys TOKEN with the BUCK: its lien becomes BUCK inventory in the TOKEN/BUCK pools, which is exactly where BVIB is priced. |
