Debtors, whose BUCK lien no longer appreciates, now gain: -$19.1k to +$36.0k against the counterfactual, six of eight positive (the historical path: six of eight negative), which confirms recommendation 51. Savers drew no credit and sit within $1k of the counterfactual. 32 recorded errors in 30,241 observations, no faults; supply equals open principal exactly. |
| 45 | all three years: the basket's lien day by day against the sign of BVIB | The basket issues on almost every day, including the 330 to 353 days a year when the BUCK is cheap against it (BVIB > 1): reverting path +4.63M of lien on those days (13k a day) and +0.69M on the 11 days at or below 1 (63k a day); historical +5.39M and +0.30M; zero tolerance +3.22M and +0.71M. Over the year the basket's lien grows about 0.7 BUCK per BUCK of new equity (reverting: equity +7.76M, lien +5.32M; households deposited 7.29M), so roughly 70% of the households' BUCK demand returns to the TOKEN/BUCK pools as the basket's own new supply. Per day the issuance is counter-cyclical (five times faster when the BUCK is dear), but it never stops while the BUCK is cheap. |

**** Recommendations

44. /Count marks taken without relief./ A campaign should report how many
    household-days had =relief_unavailable=; if they cluster after shocks the
    fund's mark guard is doing its job, but net-worth charts must say so.
45. /Quote what the contract will do, from the contract's own terms./ Two
    of the first world's three failure kinds were a household or snapshot
    using a neighbouring number (the TWAP share price; an always-available
    relief quote). Any new minimum must be derived from the same marks the
    contract checks, or from a simulation as the owner.
46. /Refinancing is fast./ Joined debtors take a tranche every day, so most
    of a debtor's convertible mortgage is refinanced within weeks, limited by
    pool depth (0.5% a bite) and utilization. In a five-year run the supply
    jump happens in the first months; a shock scheduled at day 730 meets a
    population already mostly converted. Consider a cell with a slower
    adoption cadence to see the transition itself.
47. /Saver credit needs the carry line or a premium./ With the default
    =carry_utilization = 0= savers never borrow unless the BUCK is dear by
    =issue_premium=; the first month had no premium. Decide the centre cell's
    =carry_utilization= deliberately (the owner's call); recommendation 33
    says how long a line takes to fill.
48. /Name every revert./ =IssuancePending= and the basket operations report
    "needs transaction reconciliation" or "basket operation reverted"
    without the contract's error. Decode the custom error from the receipt
    (the selector is in the ABIs) into the refusal event, so a campaign can
    count refusals by cause (=CreditExceeded=, =PremiumLimitExceeded=,
    =Slippage=, =MinOut=, =MarkPriceDeviation=).
49. /Watch K's drift before the long runs./ At -0.07 a quarter the centre
    population would take K to its floor within about a year unless debtor
    conversion saturates (it should: their mortgages are finite and K cuts
    their limits) or BUCK demand (investors holding, savers saving, wallets)
    catches up. Run 365 days next and chart K against cumulative debtor
    conversion and investor holdings; if K is still falling at the end, the
    centre cell's population mix (debtor and investor rates) or the
    controller's gains need the owner's attention before a five-year
    campaign is meaningful.
50. /Before any five-year campaign: the basket's deployment loop./ The
    year's K decline with BVIB never back at 1 (observation 39) comes with
    72% of new supply being the basket's own lien (observation 41). Who
    holds the BUCK (=doc/review/who-holds.py= found the earlier K runaway
    this way), then the same year without investors and with half the
    investor rate, will show whether investor deposits feed basket issuance
    that keeps the BUCK cheap. This is the owner's question before choosing
    the centre cell: the basket deploying deposits by issuing up to K times
    equity (the 2026-10-11 deployment ideal) is a design choice, and in this
    population it is the largest single source of supply.
51. /Debtors carry the inflation risk./ With commodities rising in USD the
    BUCK rises too, and a debtor's BUCK lien costs more dollars to repay.
    The households' =risk= setting prices this (0 to 1% a year); the year
    shows losses of several percent of the lien. Report each debtor's
    BUCK/USD exposure beside its net worth, and consider whether =risk=
    should be drawn wider or depend on observed commodity drift.
52. /The investor tolerance may set where BVIB rests./ The centre was
    balanced on legacy accounting by investors /holding/ BUCK as money
    (=economy.toml=: "only BUCKs held are demand"). Under the owner's
    2026-11-13 rule an investor deposits whenever BVIB is within its
    tolerance, the basket redeploys deposits by issuing, and only investors
    whose tolerance is below the current excess hold. That would make BVIB
    settle near 1 plus a typical tolerance (it sat at 1.01 to 1.03 all year),
    a standing error the integral controller answers by lowering K
    indefinitely. Tested (observation 42): pinning every tolerance to 0 slows
    K's fall by a third but does not stop it, and BVIB still rests above 1
    on 91% of days. The tolerance is a contributor, not the cause.
53. /The centre's K decline is structural; decide the basket's deployment
    before step D./ Neither the investors' tolerance (observation 42) nor
    commodity inflation (observation 44) causes the year-long fall: the
    supply side outruns BUCK demand in every variant, and the largest
    supply is the basket issuing about 0.7 BUCK per BUCK deposited, on cheap
    days too (observation 45), into the very pools that price BVIB. On
    legacy accounting the household-economy plan found the opposite
    (=doc/review/household-economy-plan.org=, the 2026-10-17 entry): a BUCK
    deposit repaid the machine's lien and the machine bought TOKEN only when
    a pair separated from its reference, so deposits were BUCK demand. First
    find which difference turned deposits into issuance on canonical
    accounting (where a deposit lands, or how the upkeep sizes the book
    against equity). Then the owner's choices, each to try for a year
    before any campaign: (a) the basket does not issue against new equity while
    BVIB > 1 (deployment follows the same signal as K); (b) it issues up to
    K x equity as now, and the population's investor rate rises until
    demand balances (the centre was calibrated on legacy accounting, where
    deposits were not redeployed); (c) accept a falling K as the
    equilibrium's answer and let the five-year runs show where it settles.
54. /Name the controlled gap in the charts./ BVIB is the controlled
    variable, not BUCK/USD: in the reverting year the BUCK held its dollar
    price while BVIB sat 1 to 2% above 1. Every campaign chart that shows
    BUCK/USD should show BVIB beside it, and the digest's holder split
    (TOKEN/BUCK pools, BUCK/USDC pool, basket, everyone else) beside
    supply.

** Run-record milestone: one record, the dashboard's Overview (R1 complete)

The owner's direction of 2026-11-14 (step F): one format, location and
interface for every test world, simulation and sandbox, watched through one
dashboard; Python first and local; the in-browser Tevm world kept as the
scalable public path. This is phase R1.

- [X] /The run record./ =alberta_buck/sim/runrecord.py=, schema =buck-run/1=:
  =manifest.json= (rewritten atomically: status, revision and whether the
  tree had changes, scenario, tokens, contracts from the deployment manifest,
  cast, units, outputs, capabilities) and four append-only JSON Lines files.
  =frame_record= groups a snapshot frame by component (=control=, =market=,
  =supply= with sources and holders, =basket=, =jubilee=, =population=,
  =households=, =health=); absent sources are null, never zero. Money in raw
  integers, prices and ratios as floats. Readers take whole lines only.
- [X] /Every Python run records./ =python -m alberta_buck.sim= writes
  =build/runs/<out-stem or experiment>-<time>/= (=--record DIR=,
  =--no-record=, =--run-id=; =$SIM_RUNS=); a failed run is closed =failed=
  with its error. Ids generated in the same second get a counter; a chosen id
  (a campaign cell) keeps an unfinished earlier attempt beside it and never
  replaces a complete one. Campaign cells record under
  =build/sim/economy/<campaign>/records/<cell>=. =loop.run= takes a
  =recorder=.
- [X] /Earlier runs open too./ =python -m alberta_buck.sim.runrecord import
  <vector>...= (=make nix-venv-run-import VECTORS=...=) writes a record from
  a vector (its revision unrecorded, its time the file's); re-importing the
  same vector replaces only its own view.
- [X] /The dashboard./ =alberta_buck/sim/dashboard.py= (=make
  nix-venv-dashboard=, =127.0.0.1:8890=; refuses the tunnel's ports) serves
  =core/js/dashboard/= and a read-only API (=/api/runs=, =manifest=,
  =frames?since==, =interventions=), extending each run's frames from the
  last whole line. The page: a run picker, status (running, stale after two
  minutes without an update, complete, failed), the component tabs named
  (only the Overview built), a time-range row, eight headline figures and
  six charts (K; BVIB with BUCK/USD about 1; who holds the BUCK; supply by
  source; households by role; refusals and faults), interventions marked on
  each, one crosshair across all charts, the tooltip on the hovered one,
  keyboard stepping, a table view per chart, light and dark from a validated
  palette, a phone layout. A running record is polled every two seconds.
- [X] /One source interface./ =core/js/dashboard/src/sources.js=: a source
  gives =manifest=, =frames(since)=, =interventions= and its
  =capabilities= (=live=, =control=, =agents=, =events=); tabs ask before
  offering anything. =HttpRunSource= now; a =BrowserWorldSource= (R4) and the
  live session's control (R2) plug in without a new page.
- [X] /The shape is pinned for both languages./ =test/fixtures/run-record.json=:
  snapshot frames and the records they make; =alberta_buck/test/test_runrecord.py=
  proves the Python recorder writes them, =core/js/test/dashboard-series.test.js=
  reads them (and is part of =make nix-core-equilibrium-policy-test=). A
  JavaScript world must reproduce them (R4).
- [X] /The fault counter reaches the frames./ =household_faults= was counted
  but never captured (observation 46); the snapshot records it now and the
  digest counts dispatcher-fault events where the counter is absent.
- [X] /Screenshots./ =core/js/dashboard/bin/screenshot.mjs <url> <out.png>
  [light|dark] [width]= captures the page and, separately, one chart under the
  pointer, and prints the page's console errors.

*** Checkpoint

- *Evidence*: the supported Python set with =test_runrecord.py= (nineteen
  files), 309 passed (=build/test/q5-python.log=); JavaScript policy units
  with =dashboard-series.test.js=, 83 of 83. A 20-day and a 60-day economy
  world recorded themselves; the page followed the 60-day one live; light,
  dark and phone captures showed no console error and one tooltip per hover.
  No JavaScript world code and no contract changed, so the Tevm files were
  not re-run (132 of 132 at =c8c6c278=).

*** Observations and recommendations (run records)

**** Measured

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 46 | the three one-year vectors | =household_faults= (the dispatcher's fault counter, =ctr=) was never written into a frame, so the digest's "faults 0" was a default. The households' event logs settle it: no =kind= =act= event in any of the three years, so there were no faults. Since this milestone the frame carries the counter. |
| 47 | =make nix-core-eqsim JS_EQ_ARGS="--days 30 --policy adaptive"= (one debtor, one basket saver, Tevm in Node) | Completes: 30 days in 41 minutes (about 80 s a day), no journal mismatches. The debtor refinanced its whole mortgage on the opened loop (mortgage 0, 223,134 BUCK drawn, 11.5k dollars banked); K 0.7527, BVIB 0.997, BUCK/USD 0.981 at the end. The JavaScript act loop runs end to end outside its tests. |
| 48 | the reverting year's record (=build/runs/economy-365-revert=, imported) | Frames 563 kB (1.5 kB a day); agents 8.8 MB (24 kB a day at 80 to 131 households); events 27.4 MB for 16,276 events, 63% of it the per-event account snapshot (=accounts=). Importing the three years took 4.4 s. A five-year world of 200 households would write about 3 MB of frames, 70 MB of agent lines and 300 MB of events. |
| 49 | =snapshot.write= | The vector is rewritten whole every 25 days as a checkpoint; at a year it is 100 to 124 MB, so a five-year run writes several gigabytes of checkpoints, each serializing every earlier frame. The append-only record costs one line a day. |
| 50 | a 60-day economy world recording while the dashboard watched (=build/runs/economy-growing-check=) | The page advanced from day 13 to day 18 in 20 seconds without a reload (about 4 s a simulated day at 42 to 48 households on pyrevm), showing "Running"; it then completed with K 0.752 -> 0.711, supply +2.35M (basket lien +0.83M, household principal +1.52M), BVIB above 1 on 93% of days: the year's pattern from the first weeks. |

**** Recommendations

55. /Watch the first campaign live./ The centre's K decline is visible on
    the Overview within its first weeks (observation 50). Start the next
    centre run with the dashboard open; R2's controls then let the owner try
    recommendation 53's choices on a running world before committing a
    campaign to one.
56. /Keep the browser world small, and measure it first./ One debtor and one
    saver cost about 80 s a simulated day on Tevm in Node (observation 47),
    mostly the work wheel's ticks; a visitor will not wait 40 minutes for a
    month. Before R4, profile a day (=bin/tevm-membench.mjs=) and give the
    in-tab world a lighter cast (fewer wheel turns a day, fewer pools), then
    record what its source can and cannot show in its capabilities.
57. /Trim event snapshots before five-year campaigns./ Write an event's
    =accounts= only when they changed since that household's previous event,
    or move them to their own file; that cuts the event log by about half
    (observation 48) without losing the drill-down.
58. /Retire the vector's checkpoint rewrite after R5./ Once the PDF and the
    judge read records, the 25-day full rewrite (observation 49) can stop;
    until then a five-year campaign's disk and time budget must include it.
59. /The savings sandbox shares the path./ When the session server writes
    records (R2), the public savings tab and the dashboard can read the same
    frames; the public server stays read- and sign-only, and operator control
    stays on localhost.

** Live-control milestone: steer a running world from the dashboard (R2 complete)

Step F, phase R2. The session server's port (8797) is one of the tunnel's, so
local sessions run inside the dashboard's own server instead of beside it.

- [X] /Sessions in the dashboard./ =dashboard.LiveSessions= starts a
  =server.Session= (its own thread and pyrevm EVM) from an experiment file
  with days, seed, price path and =--set= lines, optionally paused at day 0;
  kept until its operator stops it (=keep=: no reaping for want of
  watchers); at most =--max-sessions= (2) at once; only when the dashboard
  binds a loopback address. API: =/api/experiments=, =/api/sessions=
  (list, start), =/api/sessions/<sid>= (state: day, paused, pace, run, ops
  applied, queued, last result, error), =.../control=, =.../members=,
  =.../experiment=. A POST must be =application/json= from the page's own
  origin (a cross-origin page cannot send one without a preflight the
  server never answers).
- [X] /Sessions record themselves./ =server.Session= takes =record_root=:
  each world writes =live-<sid>= (a reset closes it =reset= and the rebuilt
  world writes =live-<sid>-r1=, ...); =stopped=, =failed= and =complete=
  close it otherwise. The manifest's =capabilities.control= is the
  session's address.
- [X] /Operator actions are interventions./ Ops that change the world go
  through =experiment.Interventions.apply_now= on the day given and are
  recorded with =source= =operator= (the run record's =interventions.jsonl=,
  the vector's =interventions_applied=); a refused one is recorded with its
  note and reported to the page, not raised. The server's =population=,
  =knob=, =shock= and =chain= ops map to =set_count=, =set_knob=,
  =arm_shock= and =set_chain=; =intervene= carries any action. New actions:
  =add_household= (=HouseholdPopulationAgent.add_member=: the population's
  settings with overrides, named =<population>/<role>/op<k>=, drawn and
  validated by its own setup), =household_settings=
  (=credit_households.apply_live_settings=, all or none, over
  =LIVE_SETTINGS=: utilization, carry utilization, theta, risk, issue
  premium, retire discount, BVIB tolerance, decision days, money months,
  impact cap, and the surplus policy), =set_count=, =set_chain=. Steering
  (pause, resume, step, pace, reset, stop) is recorded with =replay= false.
- [X] /Export and exact replay./ =experiment.to_toml= writes a resolved
  experiment that =load= reads back unchanged; =Session.experiment_toml=
  adds the operator's applied actions on their days. The loop now fires due
  interventions twice a day: the scenario's before the morning's arrivals,
  the exported operator actions after them (=apply_due(day,
  operator=True)=), where the live operator acted.
- [X] /The page./ "New live session" (experiment, days, seed, prices, start
  paused, extra settings); a control row (state, pause or resume, +1, +7,
  +30 days, pace, export, reset and stop, each of the last two a two-click
  button with no browser dialog); forms for a commodity price shock, a BUCK
  shock (refused when the cast has no =ShockAgent=, and the form says so),
  population and economy, adding a household, and changing a household's
  settings (current values as placeholders); the last result. Operator
  actions are marked on the charts; steering is not. The page follows a
  reset to the new record and a session's own state (paused, running)
  outranks its record's age. =src/live.js= is the control object the page
  talks to; a world in the browser tab can supply one with fewer actions.
- [X] /Checks./ =alberta_buck/test/test_dashboard_live.py= (live settings,
  the TOML round trip, a 4-day economy session steered, recorded, exported,
  reset and stopped through the API; loopback only);
  =core/js/dashboard/bin/live-check.mjs= (=make
  nix-dashboard-live-check=) drives the page itself.

*** Checkpoint

- *Evidence*: the supported Python set with =test_dashboard_live.py= (twenty
  files), 313 passed (=build/test/q7-python.log=); JavaScript policy and
  dashboard units 83 of 83; =make nix-dashboard-live-check= against a running
  dashboard: started, household added, a week run, price shock applied,
  stopped, two markers, no console error; the public savings sandbox's
  acceptance (=make nix-venv-sandbox-savings-test=, which shares
  =server.Session=) 1 of 1; the exact-replay comparison of observation 51.
  No contract or JavaScript world code changed, so the Tevm files were not
  re-run (132 of 132 at =c8c6c278=).

*** Observations and recommendations (live control)

**** Measured

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 51 | an 8-day economy session: a debtor added at day 0 (home $600k, LTV 0.5); at day 3 one debtor's utilization 0.6 and decision days 2, and investor arrivals 90 a year; exported, then replayed by =python -m alberta_buck.sim= | *The replay is exact*: all 8 frames equal in every group, all 344 household-day lines and all 480 state-machine events equal, the three actions applied on the same days. The first design applied exported actions before the morning's arrivals; the second pass after them was needed for the order of agents to match the live session. |
| 52 | the page, a 30-day economy session started paused (=live-check.mjs=) | Started, a saver added at day 0 (home $400k), seven days run, LABR's reference cut to 0.7 at day 7, one more day: BVIB fell from about 1.025 to 0.979 and BUCK/USD to 0.951 that day, four households were refused, and K rose the same day (0.748 -> 0.754). Both operator actions are marked on every chart; no console error. |
| 53 | the API test | A paused session applies an action within a poll (the paused loop drains its queue every 50 ms); an economy world builds in about 5 s; the whole 4-day test, including a reset and its rebuild, takes about 20 s. |
| 54 | =economy.toml= | The centre casts no =ShockAgent=, so a BUCK shock is refused (recorded, reported, not raised); the savings server adds one with =--set scenario.agents.ShockAgent=1=, and so can the new-session form's extra settings. |

**** Recommendations

60. /Try recommendation 53's choices live before any campaign./ Start the
    centre paused, run a quarter, change one thing (investor arrivals, a
    household's =carry_utilization=, a commodity shock), run on, export;
    each export is a cell that replays exactly, so the exploratory sessions
    become the campaign's first cells.
61. /Give the Agents tab (R3) a path to the control forms./ Choosing a
    household there should open "Change a household's settings" for it, and
    its event timeline should show operator actions beside its own steps.
62. /Keep operator actions to the engine's vocabulary./ An action that is
    not an intervention cannot be exported or replayed. New disturbances
    (the broad sell-off and purchase, raids, LP exits) belong in
    =experiment.py= first (R5), then on the page.
63. /The public savings server./ Its shock and chain ops now go through the
    same engine (same day, same effect, now listed in the vector's
    =interventions_applied=); it still writes no run record. Recording there
    needs a disk budget per visitor and a retention rule first.


** Component-tabs milestone: BuckBasket, Market, Jubilee fund, Agents (R3, part)

The owner's discussion of 2026-11-14 (the economy section near the top) asks
whether K is finding a natural level and whether the basket answers it; these
tabs are how to see that in any run, finished or live.

- [X] /The record carries the tabs' data./ The snapshot reads the basket's
  own =headroom()= and =markNow()= each day (older records: limit estimated
  as K x equity + held BUCK, the net-mark formula); =frame_record= adds them and the
  basket's unpaid relief, carried fee, held weights against the upkeep's targets and
  declared weights, and the wheel's cumulative trades; the market's BUCK in
  each TOKEN/BUCK pool and the arbitrageurs' cumulative trades. The shared
  fixture and both tests carry them. Earlier vectors re-import with them
  (=runrecord import=; a run's own record is replaced by the import of the
  vector it wrote, once it has finished).
- [X] /One household's history without loading the whole log./
  =dashboard.LineIndex= keeps byte ranges per household for =agents.jsonl=
  and =events.jsonl=, extended from the last whole line, and each one's
  latest line: =/api/runs/<id>/agents= (the table), =agents?member==,
  =events?member==.
- [X] /The tabs./ A generic chart tab (=ChartTab=) for Overview, Market,
  BuckBasket and Jubilee fund, specified in =series.js= (the BuckBasket tab's
  first chart is the lien against the contract's limit, lien + headroom; the
  second, what K bounds, (lien - held) / equity, against K) (=MARKET(tokens)=,
  =BASKET(tokens)=, =JUBILEE()=, =tabTiles=; daily changes of cumulative
  counters by =perDay=); the Agents tab (=agents.js=): a sortable, filtered
  table and a drill-down (three charts and the state-machine timeline),
  linked to the live settings form. Only the shown tab is redrawn; the tab is
  kept in the URL. Six categorical slots, validated in both modes.

*** Checkpoint

- *Evidence*: the supported Python set (twenty files), 313 passed
  (=build/test/q9-python.log=); JavaScript policy and dashboard units 85 of
  85; =make nix-dashboard-live-check= clean after the last page change
  (=q10-live.log=); light captures of the four new tabs with no console error
  and one tooltip per hover; the contract's mark measured against equity +
  held BUCK / K in two worlds (F2, observation 59).

*** Observations and recommendations (component tabs)

**** Measured

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 55 | BuckBasket tab, both one-year worlds | Lien per BUCK of equity flat at 0.82 to 0.85 from day 30 to the end while K falls from 0.75 to 0.47 (reverting) or 0.45 (history); the lien less the BUCK the basket holds in its pool positions is below zero all year (-0.076 and -0.059 of equity at the end), far from the K it would have to reach to bind (F2). Held weights stay within about 1.5 percentage points of the upkeep's targets; the share price ends at 1.009 (reverting). |
| 56 | Jubilee fund tab, reverting year | The basket's unpaid relief grows steadily to 169k BUCK (about 2% a year on its average lien, as accrued), while the fund has 6k of relief available: its cash is 60k and its basket position is marked at 506k. The basket earns relief that is not being paid. |
| 57 | Market tab, reverting year | Triangle gaps swing about +-2% day to day and end at 0.39% at most; the USDC pools stay within about +-1% of their references; the BUCK in the BUCK/USDC pool stays near 10M all year while the TOKEN/BUCK pools grow from 11.1M to 17.7M (observation 43). |
| 58 | Agents tab, reverting year, the household furthest below its counterfactual | 0/debtor/7 arrived on day 330, drew 478k BUCK within its limit in about 30 days, and paid its dollar mortgage down from about 700k to 270k; it ends 19.1k dollars below its counterfactual, its switching costs and the conversion's price impact not yet repaid. Its 157 steps (assessments declined, exports, mortgage payments, saving deposits) read in order on the page. |
| 59 | 30 days from K0 = 0.40 (=build/runs/underwater-k040=, recording the contract's headroom and mark) | Expected the basket to open under water (its lien is 0.43 of its gross mark); it opened with +2.48M of headroom and ended at +3.09M, K 0.402 -> 0.388, BVIB above 1 on most days. The mark was 1.75 to 1.81 times the gross, exactly equity + held BUCK / K: as K falls the mark rises, so the limit falls only on the small lien the basket no longer holds. |
| 60 | the centre year from K0 = 0.5 on both price paths (=economy-365-k050{,-revert}=; re-imported records) | *No natural K from below either.* History: K 0.502 -> 0.249, BVIB above 1 on 95% of days, supply +6.37M of which the basket's lien +5.10M (80%), household principal +1.27M; BUCK/USD 1.016 -> 1.109. Reverting: K 0.502 -> 0.276, BVIB above 1 on 93%, supply +7.31M, basket lien +5.98M (82%). The lien less held BUCK ended at -0.054 and -0.065 of equity: K never bound the basket. Debtors drew half as much as from 0.75 (1.27M and 1.32M against 2.17M and 2.59M), so a lower K slowed the households and not the basket; households' net worth against the counterfactual -$9.4k to +$5.2k (history), -$0.7k to +$24.9k (reverting). |

**** Recommendations

64. /The basket's relief accrues because it never retires principal
    (observation 56)./ Relief is paid on retirement or renewal; the
    basket's retirement job is deployed off (=retirementCap= 0). Turning it on
    (=deploy.basket_retirement_cap_m=) pays the relief in chunks of at most
    10% of the fund's cash; whether the basket's equity should count unpaid
    relief remains a design question. A five-year run would carry about five
    times the backlog if left off.
65. /The Events tab next./ Refusals by decoded cause across all households
    and days (recommendation 48 first: decode each revert's custom error into
    the event), and a list of the latest refused steps linking to the
    household.
66. /The Insurers tab needs reads first./ The frames do not record the
    insurers' pools: add each pool's premium deposits, coverage, facilities
    and liquidity limit to =net_snapshot.observe=, then the tab.
67. /Many runs on datasim.local./ Point a dashboard on that machine at a
    campaign's =records= directory and reach it through an SSH tunnel (live
    control stays loopback-only); then add a listing over several runs
    directories grouped by campaign, and a =control.jsonl= channel in each
    run directory so batch cells can be steered like dashboard sessions.
68. /Owner's question: does K bound the basket's held BUCK?/ Under the
    net-mark policy (=_creditValueS=) the BUCK the basket issued and holds as
    the BUCK side of its own pool positions is exempt from K, and in every
    world that is more than its whole lien (F2, observation 59). Those BUCK sit
    in the TOKEN/BUCK pools, where TOKEN sellers receive them and BVIB is
    priced. If K is to be the economy's lever, it has to reach them, or the
    basket's response to K has to be written another way (the build list's
    item 2). This is the first thing to settle before the five-year runs.

69. /Decompose who holds BVIB above 1./ The basket is net long BUCK while
    the BUCK is cheap, yet BVIB stays 1 to 3% above 1 all year. Record each
    actor class's BUCK bought and sold a day (debtors' conversions,
    investors' purchases, arbitrageurs, the wheel, the market maker; the
    snapshot's =mx.cls= has per-class volumes) and chart them on the Market
    tab: the persistent seller should be visible, and so whether the gap is
    conversion pressure or the arbitrage cost band (which an integral
    controller would also chase without end).

** Exit milestone: basket exits always proceed, BUCK by default (contract change; Solidity done, worlds next)

The owner's decisions of 2026-11-14 (the economy section, "The owner's
decisions on exits, relief and the reserve"), implemented tests first.

- [X] /The exit rule./ =BuckBasketEquity=: =redeem= / =redeemShares= pay BUCK:
  from the wallet when it can pay and still fund the principal the exit may
  retire (K x the payment); else =BasketMarketOps.exitAssets= (the basket's
  linked library, run in its own context) withdraws the receipt's fraction of
  every position and idle TOKEN and sells the TOKEN through each constituent's
  pool (=monetaryLeg=, inside the venue's TWAP band), paying what was released
  and fetched less the receipt's lien share; sold short of that share it
  reverts =Underwater=; =minBuck= bounds the slippage. New =redeemInKind(id,
  shares)= delivers TOKEN by transfer (the old behaviour). Legacy (non-net)
  accounting keeps its old path.
- [X] /The one gate./ =_exitSnapshot= no longer refuses an under-water basket;
  it returns the shortfall. After the position lock is released,
  =_holdShortfall= retires (via =this.retireCredit=) exactly the principal by
  which the exit grew the K shortfall, then requires it no larger and marks.
  =redeemTreasury= follows the same path.
- [X] /Backing after retirement./ BUCK's settlement deactivates coverage in
  proportion to principal retired; =retireCredit= now re-activates the
  basket's free self-insured backing at once (no cash, principal or age), so a
  retirement no longer shrinks its collateral (this also changes the wheel's
  retirement job and explicit retirements).
- [X] /Size./ The shell is 24,524 bytes (52 under the limit) after moving the
  asset path into =BasketMarketOps= (now about 20.2 kB). No deployment change:
  the shell already links the library.
- [X] /Tests./ =test/basket/BuckCompoundBasketExit.t.sol= rewritten (15 tests:
  under-water exits retire what holds the shortfall; an exit that would
  create one retires the excess; a fuzz pinning the exact retirement; BUCK by
  default with TOKEN sold; in kind on request; the conversion minimum rolls
  everything back; a near-zero K pool exit; unpaid relief is not
  collateral; treasury exits under water). Three others updated to the new
  rules (=BuckCompoundBasketRepayment=, =BuckCompoundBasketFlow=,
  =JubileeEquityIntegration=: the fund's sale is now paid BUCK and needs no
  TOKEN route).
- [X] /Docs./ The shell's NatSpec, =alberta-buck-ethereum.org= ("Redemption";
  invariant E4 restated, E7 added), =doc/BASKET-MACHINE.org=,
  =doc/BASKET-MACHINE-TASKS.org= (the in-kind question answered),
  =alberta-buck-ethereum-wheel.org=.
- [X] /Qualified in the simulations./ =make nix-sim-build= and the JS bundle;
  the twenty-file Python set (313 passed); the JS policy units (85 of 85)
  and the Tevm files (132 of 132); a 30-day economy world, identical to the pre-change one;
  a 45-day world with half the investors leaving on day 20.
- [X] /The one-year world./ =build/sim/exits-365.json= (run record
  =build/runs/exits-365=) against =economy-smoke-365=: observation 68.
- [ ] /Still to do./ SDK wrappers for =redeemInKind= (Python =basket.py=, JS
  =basket.js=) if any agent wants them; then the contracts release.

*** Checkpoint

*** Observations and recommendations (exits)

**** Measured

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 61 | the centre year with the basket's retirement job on (=deploy.basket_retirement_cap_m=0.1=, =build/runs/economy-365-retire=) | Identical to the default year (K 0.4451, the same supply and holders): the job never ran. It retires only from cash above the liquidity target, and the wheel deploys every spare BUCK before any accumulates. |
| 62 | the exit tests | Retiring principal through BUCK's settlement deactivates coverage in proportion, so the first implementation's retirement left the shortfall larger than before; re-activating the free backing in =retireCredit= fixed it, and the fuzz now pins the exact retirement: max(0, lien - K x (equity after paying) - before). |
| 63 | the 45-day withdrawal world | Half the investors leaving at once completed every exit in BUCK (447k over 14 redemptions); none needed the in-kind path and none reverted. Their sales of TOKEN through the pools moved BUCK/USD down to 0.953 before the wheel and arbitrage pulled it back: the leavers' slippage is the remaining holders' gain, as the owner expected, but it is a visible price event. |
| 68 | the one-year economy world on the new exits (=exits-365=) against =economy-smoke-365= | The two runs are the same event for event until day 33, when debtor 0 makes the year's first partial redemption: the old rules paid it 450 BUCK and four constituents in kind, the new 6,954 BUCK, so it paid principal down at once (28 repayment events in the year against 90) and the debtors' "repayment owner-funds" errors fell from 30 to 2 (all errors 101 to 78, faults 0 in both). The rest follows from that, through the arrivals the price gap draws: 140 households against 150 (125 investors against 135), supply +7.12M against +7.91M BUCK (basket lien +5.23M against +5.69M), K 0.4638 against 0.4451 at the end, BUCK/USD 1.107 against 1.109, BVIB above 1 on 97% of days in both. Debtors' net worth against their counterfactual ranges -18.8k to +4.5k dollars against -25.5k to +4.9k; savers' -0.4k to +7.8k against -0.7k to +8.4k. No household exited in either year, so the full-exit path itself is exercised only by the 45-day withdrawal world (observation 63). |

**** Recommendations

70. /Give the retirement job a trigger that can fire./ "Cash above the
    liquidity target" never happens in a deployed basket (observation 61).
    If the owner wants the basket to pull BUCK out of circulation as K falls
    (H3), the job needs a different trigger: retire when the basket is under
    water, or when it is net long BUCK beyond its target, from the BUCK side
    of its positions.
71. /Exits move prices: measure them./ A forced sale is bounded by the venue's
    TWAP band per call, and the leaver's =minBuck=. In the worlds, record each
    exit's slippage and the wheel's capture afterwards (the owner's expected
    transfer to the remaining holders), on the BuckBasket tab.

** In-browser engine milestone: what Tevm spends its time on, and webevm + revm instead

The owner asked (2026-11-14) why Tevm is so slow, whether retained history is the
cause, and whether anyone else has an EVM that runs entirely in a browser.

- [X] /Profiler./ =core/js/src/tevm-profile.js= times every action a session's
  client is asked for (viem binds actions in closures, so wrapping
  =client.request= alone saw only the direct =evm_mine= calls) by action and
  by contract function; =eqsim.mjs --profile FILE= writes it with the day
  timings and, on Tevm, the retained history per day (=--prune= prunes and
  trims daily).
- [X] /History is not the cost./ Four days, stock against pruned daily: 107,
  149, 125, 181 s against 107, 146, 121, 174 s, while the kept state roots stay
  at 9 instead of growing to 218 and the storage slots at 678 instead of 1,140.
  The state stays about 1.1 MB either way (=build/test/prof-stock.json=,
  =prof-prune.json=).
- [X] /What it is./ A CPU profile of one day (=node --cpu-prof=): 69% of the
  CPU in the garbage collector, 14% in viem's hex conversions, 5% in
  =@tevm/state=. Every call-frame commit runs =dumpCanonicalGenesis=, which
  re-encodes every account's whole code as a hex string; every read and
  every mined block copies the VM, and =generateCanonicalGenesis= parses that
  hex back into bytes. About 1 MB of the 1.1 MB state is contract bytecode, so
  each read and each frame allocates and discards megabytes of strings. By
  action (=prof-actions.json=, two days): =writeContract= averages 2.0 s (the
  controller's =tick= 4.6 s, up to 18 s), =readContract= 254 ms, mining
  0.23 s.
- [X] /The one-line fix, tested./ Memoising code hex to bytes and back
  (bytecode never changes) in a patched copy of =@tevm/state=, loaded through
  a Node resolve hook (=core/js/node_modules/.cache/tevm-patch/=, not
  installed): two days in 27 s instead of 234 s (8.7x), identical K, BVIB and
  BUCK/USD; with the earlier fast-commit patch as well, 20.5 s (11.4x). Worth
  an upstream issue; not adopted, because of the next item.
- [X] /Upstream Tevm./ The checkout at =~/src/tevm= (rc.153) has replaced the
  EthereumJS engine with ZEVM, a Zig EVM loaded as a native Node addon: fast,
  but it does not run in a browser, and its WASM adapter is not implemented.
  The published 1.0.0-next.149 we use is the last JavaScript one.
- [X] /The survey./ Of the in-browser EVMs found (webevm, guillotine-mini,
  EthereumJS v10 alone, Solenoid/YEVM, SputnikVM), webevm
  (github.com/wighawag/webevm, MIT, npm =webevm= 0.9.1) is the one that is a
  whole node: an execution-only EIP-1193 node on =@ethereumjs/vm= with a flat
  state (no copy per read; checkpoint and revert), an optional revm-wasm
  engine reading the node's state through host callbacks, Worker hosting and
  IndexedDB persistence, a conformance battery against =ethereum/tests=, and
  playwright tests on Chromium and WebKit. Its one gap for our worlds was time:
  mined blocks took the wall clock (or one fixed =blockEnv.timestamp=), with no
  =evm_setNextBlockTimestamp=. It holds no keys (no =eth_sendTransaction=),
  which our sessions never needed: they sign locally.
- [X] /The fork./ =~/src/webevm= (origin =pjkundert/webevm=, upstream
  =wighawag/webevm=), branch =feature/block-clock-cheats=: the node's clock
  with =evm_setNextBlockTimestamp=, =evm_increaseTime=,
  =evm_setBlockTimestampInterval= / =evm_removeBlockTimestampInterval= and
  =evm_mine= (with an optional timestamp); unchanged when no cheat is issued;
  a battery run on both engines and both browsers (the executed =TIMESTAMP=,
  intervals, a refused transaction keeps the next timestamp, =loadState= drops
  it, refusals), ADR 0015, README, glossary, changeset. Written to the
  repository's own conventions so it can go upstream.
- [X] /Our side./ =core/js/src/webevm-backend.js= (=webevmSession({engine:
  "revm" | "ethereumjs"})=, Node-only for now: it reads the wasm from disk;
  =WEBEVM_DIR= names the fork's built package) and =eqsim.mjs --backend
  webevm [--engine revm]=. Every block is its parent plus one second (the
  interval), so a run's timestamps do not depend on its speed.
- [ ] /Next./ The browser: a bundle of the sandbox on webevm (the wasm from its
  URL, a Worker host), replacing =tevmSession= there; the remaining Tevm-only
  calls (=tevmSetAccount=, =tevmDumpState= / =tevmLoadState=, the profile's
  retained-history probe) mapped to webevm's =evm_setAccount= and
  =dumpState= / =loadState=; the Tevm test files run on webevm; a pinned
  webevm dependency (the fork's tag, or upstream once the clock is merged
  there); memory over a five-year run (webevm keeps every block, receipt and
  log).

*** Checkpoint

- *Evidence*: the same eqsim world (one debtor, one basket saver, the arb, the
  whales, the wheel and PID keepers) on three engines. Two days: stock Tevm
  234,490 ms, webevm on EthereumJS 3,828 ms, webevm on revm 217 ms; the
  printed summaries identical apart from the time. Thirty days on webevm +
  revm: 2.4 s (Tevm: 41 minutes, observation 47) with observation 47's
  figures to the digit (K 0.7527, BVIB 0.997, BUCK/USD 0.981, mortgage
  refinanced, 223,134 BUCK drawn, 11.5k banked). One year: 31.7 s, 87 ms a
  simulated day (89 ms on day 1, 191 ms on day 180, 60 ms on day 364), no
  mismatches, 866 MB peak resident memory. The fork's two block-clock
  specs pass on Chromium and WebKit, both engines.

*** Observations and recommendations (in-browser engine)

**** Measured

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 64 | Tevm, four days stock and pruned (=build/test/prof-stock.json=, =prof-prune.json=) | Pruning Tevm's history daily keeps its memory flat and changes the time per day by under 5%: history was the memory ceiling, never the CPU one. |
| 65 | Tevm, one day under =--cpu-prof= | 69% of the CPU is garbage collection of the hex strings Tevm makes when it serialises the whole state (code included) on every call-frame commit and every read's VM copy. A memo of code hex to bytes and back alone makes it 8.7x faster with identical results. |
| 66 | webevm + revm, two days, thirty days, a year | About 1,000x faster than Tevm with identical results: a simulated day in tens of milliseconds, reads at 0.2 ms (Tevm 254 ms), the controller's =tick= at 6 ms (Tevm 4.6 s). A year of the small world is half a minute. |
| 67 | webevm on its own EthereumJS engine | 60x faster than Tevm on the same EthereumJS interpreter: the gain is mostly webevm's flat state (no state copy per read), and revm adds another 18x. |

**** Recommendations

72. /Move the in-browser world to webevm + revm./ It removes the reason the
    in-browser sandbox had to stay small (observation 47's 80 s a day): a
    visitor's browser can now run years of the canonical world in minutes, so
    the sandbox can show the same five-year runs the server does, and the
    savings sandbox can replay a saver's whole horizon. Keep Tevm only until
    the sandbox and the Tevm test files run on webevm.
73. /Offer the clock upstream./ The fork's branch is written to webevm's own
    conventions (battery, ADR, changeset); a pull request to =wighawag/webevm=
    keeps us on a maintained package rather than a fork. Until it merges, pin
    the fork by tag.
74. /Measure memory before a five-year browser run./ 866 MB resident for a
    year in Node includes every block, receipt and log webevm keeps; a browser
    tab's budget is lower. If it grows with the run, the remedy is on webevm's
    side (a bounded block store, as its =stateHistory= already bounds state).
75. /Report the code-hex memo to Tevm./ Anyone else on the JavaScript Tevm
    pays the same 8.7x; it is a small, safe patch.

*** Observations and recommendations (sandbox on webevm, five-year JS world)

**** Measured

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 69 | the sandbox on webevm (the controller gate and the page in Chromium) | The whole sandbox story runs in 7 s in Node and the page test in 11 s, and the page carries 1 MB less JavaScript. The in-browser world is no longer the thing that limits what a visitor can do. |
| 70 | five years of the JS world (=build/test/eqsim-5y.svg=) | K holds at 0.75 for about 200 days, then falls in a straight line to 0 by about day 1600, while BVIB stays 0 to 2% above 1 throughout: the controller's integral keeps lowering K and the basket observable never comes back to 1. When K reaches 0, BVIB jumps to 1.16 and BUCK/USD falls to 0.93 and stays there. The debtor ends ahead of its counterfactual. The same wind-down the Python household economy shows in a year (K 0.75 to 0.45, observation 68), in a world with one debtor. |
| 72 | five years of the JS world, each actor removed in turn (=eqsim.mjs --without=; =build/test/k5y-*.log=) | K reaches 0 within five years whether the debtor, the saver, both, or the arbitrageur is removed: with no households at all, K goes 0.75, 0.64, 0.45, 0.21, 0 at years 1 to 4 and 5. The wind-down is the machine's own (basket, wheel, PID and the commodity price walk), not the households'. |
| 73 | the same, without the commodity price walk (=--step 0=, or no whales to impose it) | BVIB hovers about 1 (0.994 to 1.010) and K ends the fifth year at 0.58 instead of 0: the walk drives most of the wind-down. What remains still accelerates (K falls 0.01, 0.02, 0.05 and 0.09 a year), so there is a second, slower cause. |
| 71 | the wheel's ticks over the five years | Per-slot gas is flat for four years (peaks 10.2M), then from day 1443, as K nears 0.15, ticks need up to 13.9M: the keeper's 12M limit is crossed only in the collapse regime. |

**** Recommendations

76. /K's wind-down is the five-year question; it is the controller and the
    walk./ Observations 72 and 73 locate it: with no households at all, a
    commodity walk keeps BVIB 0.5 to 2% above 1 and the integral lowers K
    about 0.2 a year until it reaches 0. BVIB is the basket's quantities at
    each TOKEN/BUCK pool's TWAP, and arbitrage closes a gap only to the pools'
    fees, so a trend can hold BUCK at one edge of that band; nothing in the
    machine-only world responds to K, so the integral has no lever. The
    owner's choices are in the controller and the observable (a dead band as
    wide as the arbitrage band, a bounded or leaking integral, measuring BVIB
    where a trend cannot bias it) or in giving K a lever (recommendation 68).
    The slower residual without the walk (observation 73) is a separate
    question for after that. The Python household economy winds down the
    same way (K 0.75 to 0.45 in a year), so until this is settled a
    five-year campaign measures this drift rather than the design. A small JS
    world runs five years in about three minutes (=eqsim.mjs --days 1825
    --tick-gas 29000000 --without debtor,saver=), so a candidate controller
    can be tried in minutes before the Python campaign.
77. /Give the keeper the block, not 12M./ A wheel tick near K = 0 needs 13.9M
    gas; a keeper on a real chain would estimate and pay up to the block limit.
    Raise the simulated keeper's default (or estimate per tick) once 76 is
    understood, so a world in the collapse regime records the collapse rather
    than stopping.
78. /Release blocker: the webevm dependency./ =alberta-buck-core= cannot be
    published with a =file:= dependency. Before its next release, either
    upstream has released the clock or the fork is published under a scope
    (the owner's call).

** The leaky integrator, and the bootstrap economy's actors (owner's direction, 2026-11-14)

The owner's reading of observations 70 to 73: with no actor responding to K, BVIB
wanders with prices and K wanders inversely; the leak is the right safety net, but a
real BUCK economy has actors who respond to K. A household with interest-bearing debt
issues BUCKs and retires that debt when K makes it worthwhile, then later accumulates
BUCKs to retire its liability (or the Jubilee does so over 50 years), so the system
issues and redeems BUCKs in balance. Once issuance and redemption flows balance at some
K, the integral stops moving. The task is to find the minimal set of actors, in a
realistic mix, that brings an economy up from zero: not $100,000 mortgage
redemptions on day one, but small household loans and savings first, building from
there; points programs are a later candidate for a controlled on- and off-ramp.

- [X] /The leak./ =BuckKControllerDirect.setLeak(tau)= (governance; 0 = none, the
  contract's default): each cycle the integrator first decays by =I x dt / tau=
  (first order; a cycle of tau or longer forgets it), then adds the cycle's
  increment. The integral of a sustained error e settles at e x tau, so K settles
  at K0 + Kp e + Ki e tau instead of railing, and returns to K0 at parity. Shared
  with the shadow controller (=_leakedI=). Seven new tests in
  =test/BuckKControllerDirect.t.sol= on the simulations' own gains (one cycle is
  first order; a cycle longer than tau forgets; a sustained 1% error rails the
  unleaked loop and settles the leaked one at the predicted K; parity returns K to
  K0 within 0.001 after five time constants; a leak lifts a railed K). =make
  nix-test= 1,555 passed, 0 failed.
- [X] /Defaults (owner)./ The worlds deploy a one-year leak by default
  (=deploy.leak_days= 365 in Python, =leakTau= in =core/js/src/buckworld.js=; the
  owner: at least a year, probably longer). K0 stays 0.75: the owner suspects it is
  near the natural equilibrium, and it leaves every test as it was (0.5 was tried;
  33 Python tests sized for 0.75 failed). =eqsim.mjs --k0 --leak-days=.
- [ ] /The actors, as the owner describes them./
  | actor | in the household economy today | to build |
  |-------+--------------------------------+-----------|
  | borrowers with small interest-bearing loans (cars, consumer debt) | the debtor, with insured assets of $200k to $1.5M (=home_k=, log-uniform) | the same debtor with the range scaled down; premiums proportionally higher for small loans: one straight line, the premium a rate on the insured value plus a fixed cost per policy (proposed =premium_fixed_usd=) |
  | households saving, then retiring | savers (a share of the K-scaled limit kept drawn) and investors (USD into the basket, withdrawn whole at a horizon) | one household over its life: accumulate for decades, then draw the savings down as retirement income, not one withdrawal |
  | savings outside BUCK | none | a share of each household's savings in an external USD fund returning about 8% a year over the long run: the basket's competitor for those savings |
  | issuers that respond to K | arrivals paced by (1 - k_ref) / (1 - K) and by the price gap; debtors pay down when the BUCK is cheap; asset sales settle liens (10% a year) | kept; checked against the bootstrap mix |
  | points programs | none | later: points to BUCKs and back at a controlled rate |
- [ ] /Then the bootstrap world./ An experiment that starts from an empty economy
  with small loans and savers, grows into the full mix, and runs five years; the
  measure is whether K settles (issuance and redemption balance) and where, with
  BVIB about 1.

*** Checkpoint

- *Evidence*: the five-year JS world (=eqsim.mjs --days 1825 --tick-gas
  29000000=), walk 80 bp a day. At K0 0.5 with the actors removed: no leak, K
  0.49, 0.39, 0.20, 0 at years 1 to 4 (BVIB 1.15 after); leak 90 days, K 0.43 at
  year 5; 180 days, 0.37; 365 days, 0.26 (=build/test/leak-*.log=). On the new
  defaults (K0 0.75, one-year leak): K 0.74, 0.67, 0.60, 0.55, 0.51 at years 1 to
  5, levelling, with or without the households (=leak-def-*.log=); the debtor
  ends with its mortgage refinanced and $141k banked. The Python set 331 passed
  on the new defaults; the sandbox gates 15 of 15; the JS policy units 65 of 65.

*** Observations and recommendations (leak and bootstrap)

**** Measured

| # | Fixture | Observation |
|---+---------+-------------|
| 74 | the leak sweep at K0 0.5, no households | A leak bounds K: the longer the leak, the further a persistent error carries it (0.43, 0.37 and 0.26 after five years at 90, 180 and 365 days), but never to a rail, where the unleaked loop reached 0 in four years. With BVIB about 1% above 1 for years, K settles about Ki x 1% x tau below K0, as the tests predict. |
| 75 | the new defaults, five years | K falls a quarter in five years and levels off; the one debtor and one saver of the small JS cast make no visible difference to K: they are too small to be the actors the owner means. |

**** Recommendations

79. /Build the owner's actors in this order./ The fixed-cost premium and the
    scaled loan range first (settings only, both languages); then the household
    lifecycle (accumulate, then draw down) with the external USD fund at about 8%
    as the alternative; then the bootstrap experiment from an empty economy. Each
    is measured by where K settles over five years.
80. /Lengthen the leak with evidence./ A year bounds K's wander to about
    Ki x error x one year; once the bootstrap mix balances issuance and
    redemption, the residual error should be small and the leak can lengthen
    (the owner expects longer), which the same five-year runs will show.

** The household lifecycle and the bootstrap mix (owner's direction, 2026-11-14)

The owner: shelve points programs. Build the household lifecycle (debt retirement,
investment accumulation, then retirement income) with a typical mix of young,
middle-aged and retired households; that mix should reach an equilibrium K, and
varying the proportion of each should give a different one. The economy introduces
young families at a rate relative to its existing population, and the debt load of
the incoming families grows slowly as the economy's capacity grows.

The design, fitted to the household economy as it is (roles keep their machinery;
the lifecycle decides which role a household plays and when it stops earning):

| stage | role | arrives with | does |
|-------+------+--------------+------|
| young family (25 to 40) | debtor | an insured home and a mortgage (scaled by the economy's capacity) | refinances into BUCK credit (issuance), sells for its mortgage, saves its surplus, pays down its liability |
| middle-aged (40 to 64) | saver, investor | an insured asset (saver) or savings (investor) | accumulates: a share in the BUCK basket, a share in an external USD fund (about 8% a year) |
| retired (65 on) | investor | savings in proportion to the years worked | draws its savings down as income over its remaining years, the basket and the fund in proportion, then leaves |

- [X] /Progress (2026-11-14)./ L1 to L4 built and committed (15dc8cf5, 5484849d,
  e35bb39a), with the owner's two refinements since: a retired household earns
  =pension_frac= (20%) of its former wages (a public pension such as Social
  Security or the CPP) while its living costs stay, and makes up the rest from its
  savings; and it draws the USD fund and its basket savings in proportion to their
  values (=advance_net_drawdown=, debtors and savers; a retired investor draws a
  month's share of both, =drawdown_share=). A working debtor or saver saves
  =usd_fund_share= of what its wages leave after its bills in the fund. The run
  record carries each household's age, stage, fund and fund paid out, and the
  population's counts by stage; the dashboard shows a life-stage chart, age and
  stage columns, and each household's savings (basket mark, fund, fund paid out).
  =economy-lifecycle.toml= is the lifecycle world (26 households: 4 young
  families, 4 middle-aged savers, 12 middle-aged investors, 6 retirees; young
  families arriving at 5% of the members a year; mortgages capped at 0.5% of the
  BUCK supply). Its 30-day run completes without faults. One-year runs of three
  mixes (balanced, young-heavy, retiree-heavy) started on the investor-only
  version, before the pension.
- [ ] /L1. The lifecycle in the policy./ Settings =age= (years at arrival),
  =retire_age= (65), =drawdown_years= (25), =usd_fund_share=, =usd_fund_return=
  (0.08); a household's age on a day, its stage, and the share of its savings a
  retiree draws down on a payday (1 / months left). Python and JS alike, tested
  on the shared fixture.
- [ ] /L2. Accumulation split./ An investor's saving goes =usd_fund_share= to the
  external fund (off chain, compounding at =usd_fund_return=), the rest into the
  basket as today.
- [ ] /L3. Retirement drawdown./ After =retire_age=, each payday the investor
  redeems that share of its basket shares (paid in BUCK, the new exits), sells
  the BUCK it does not spend, and takes the same share of its fund; after
  =drawdown_years= it leaves as an investor leaves today. Its wages stop.
- [ ] /L4. The population./ An initial mix by stage (young, middle-aged, retired,
  with their age ranges); young families arriving at a rate per year of the
  current population; the incoming mortgage scaled by the economy's capacity
  (the BUCK/USDC route's depth, which bounded refinancing before), from car-sized
  loans in an empty economy up to the full range.
- [ ] /L5. The equilibrium./ Five-year runs from an empty economy, varying the
  mix; where K settles (with the one-year leak) and BVIB about 1.

The JavaScript port of the household agents follows L1 (the policy is mirrored
now); its agent behaviour for L2 to L4 follows once the Python worlds show the
equilibrium.

** The savings sandbox on webevm: JS household parity first (design, 2026-11-14)

The owner: start porting the savings sandbox to webevm; keep its connection to a
Python + pyrevm world if that is simple (it is: below); bring the JS household to
parity with the Python implementation first. Tevm's speed was the only reason the
savings world lives on a server: webevm + revm runs the small JS world a year in
half a minute (observation 66).

*** Where it stands

- *The savings sandbox* is a page (=core/js/sandbox/src/ui/savings.js=,
  =sandbox/src/savings/{link,model,wallet,abi}.js=) watching a Python world
  (=economy.toml= on pyrevm) hosted by =alberta_buck/sim/server.py=. Its link is
  three websockets to one session: *frames* (lite frames, replayed from day 0 on
  connect), *control* (pause, step, pace, shock, chain; interventions since R2),
  and *JSON-RPC* (the saver's reads and signed transactions). =make
  nix-venv-sandbox-savings-test= passes (1 of 1, 81 s) on this revision.
- *It does not use the dashboard.* The dashboard (=core/js/dashboard=) reads run
  records through =alberta_buck/sim/dashboard.py=; the savings page reads the
  server's lite frames. Both descend from the same snapshot frame, so they can
  share one shape (the run record's =frame_record=, pinned by
  =test/fixtures/run-record.json= for both languages).
- *The wallet sandbox* already runs on webevm in the tab (=make
  nix-wallet-sandbox-test= 15 of 15, the page in Chromium included).
- *The JS household* has the canonical state machine (=net-household.js=,
  =credit-household.js=, =household-calendar=, the shared =household-policy=);
  the eight JS world files of the qualified set pass 132 of 132 on webevm. But:
  - its population test (=core/js/test/legacy/credit-household.webevm.test.js=)
    fails three cases on a calendar-day-against-chain-clock check that the
    earlier Permit2 failure had hidden;
  - the lifecycle (age, pension, USD fund, drawdown, stages, young-family
    arrivals, the mortgage cap) is Python only; the JS policy has its functions
    (=ageOn=, =stage=, =drawdownShare=, =fundGrowth=, the population keys), not
    the agent behaviour;
  - there is no JS world built from an experiment: =eqworld.js= builds a
    machine world with a fixed small cast, not =economy.toml='s population,
    bootstrap LPs, arbitrageurs and keepers;
  - the JS worlds write no run record (plan step R4).

*** The design

1. /One world, two hosts, one link./ The savings page talks to a *world link*
   with the three channels it has now. =ServerLink= is today's (Python +
   pyrevm, unchanged: the server stays the research and five-year host).
   =LocalLink= hosts the JS world in a Web Worker on webevm + revm: frames are
   posted per simulated day, control ops apply between days, and JSON-RPC is
   the in-tab node's own =request= (=webevm/worker-host= already serves a node
   over a =MessagePort=). The page chooses by its link: =sim-server.json='s
   =server= (as now) or local by default.
2. /The world from the experiment./ A JS world builder reads the same experiment
   (TOML converted to JSON at build time by Python's own loader, so both
   languages read one resolved experiment): the deploy parameters, the basket
   machine, the bootstrap LPs, the whales on the price path, the arbitrageurs,
   the keepers, and the household population with its settings. Shared keyed
   random streams (=rng = "keyed"=, already language-neutral) give both
   languages the same arrivals and decisions.
3. /Frames in the run record's shape./ The JS world emits =frame_record= frames
   (R4) and the agents' lines, so the savings page, the dashboard and the parity
   check all read one shape; =runrecord.py='s fixture pins it.
4. /The dashboard and the savings page converge./ The dashboard's sources take a
   local world as one more source (fewer capabilities than a server: no
   five-year runs, a page's memory); the savings page keeps its own small view.

*** Steps, in order

- [ ] /S1. Parity inventory./ Python against JS, function by function: the
  household agent (=credit_households.py= / =credit-household.js=), the state
  machine (=net_household.py= / =net-household.js=), the calendar, the
  population, the world (=deploy.py=, =basket_machine.py=, the agents in
  =equilibrium_agents.py= and =direct_mint.py= / =eqworld.js=). A table of what
  each has, what the other lacks, and the tests on each side.
- [ ] /S2. A parity harness./ The same resolved experiment and seed in both
  languages for N days, compared day by day: arrivals and decisions identical
  (keyed streams), K, BVIB, supply, holders and each household's books within
  stated tolerances; the operation journals equal where both make the same
  calls (=mini_parity= and =js_join= are the precedents). Python on pyrevm, JS
  on webevm in Node.
- [ ] /S3. Close the gaps the harness shows./ First the JS population's
  calendar-against-clock failure; then the lifecycle behaviour (the calendar's
  pension and fund saving, the investor's monthly drawdown, a retired debtor's
  or saver's proportional drawdown, the population's stages, retirees,
  young-family arrivals and mortgage cap); then whatever else S1 and S2 find.
  Done when the harness passes 30 days of =economy.toml= and
  =economy-lifecycle.toml=.
- [ ] /S4. The JS world builder and its run record (R4)./ Experiment JSON in,
  deployment and cast out; frames and agent lines in the record's shape.
- [ ] /S5. Speed./ Seconds per simulated day in Node and in Chromium for 30, 100
  and 150 households; the household count a tab can carry at a day every few
  seconds sets the local world's default population.
- [ ] /S6. =LocalLink= and the page./ The worker hosting webevm and the world;
  the three channels; the page choosing its link; =savings.browser.test.js= run
  against both links.
- [ ] /S7. The dashboard's local source./

Acceptance: the savings page runs =economy.toml= (or a smaller default) wholly in
a tab, a visitor's wallet trades in it, and the same page still attaches to a
Python server world; the parity harness passes 30 days of both worlds.

** The owner's word on the open decisions, and the sandboxes' worlds in the dashboard (2026-11-14, evening)

- *The V2 adapter: discarded* ("we don't use V2 pools anymore").
  =src/adapters/UniswapV2BindingAdapter.sol= and its eight tests are gone; the
  adapter suite tests V3 only, and gained the two checks only V2 had (governance
  revocation rolls back pool creation; no creating over an existing pool): 12 of
  12. The identity paper's admission steps and walkthrough now name the V3
  adapter (=createPoolAndBind=, the factory's =owner=). *Kept, deliberately*:
  the legacy =UniswapV2Integration= suite and its staged V2 factory, pair and
  router. It is the only end-to-end test of the identity rules through an AMM
  (verified LP, verified swaps both ways, an unverified recipient refused), on
  the legacy signed Buck; its cases move to canonical Buck on a V3 pool before
  it and the V2 staging go (consolidation wave 3).
- *The shadow controller* (=BuckKControllerShadow=, =ShadowObserver=,
  =IShadowObserver=, the sim-only =SimStabilizer=; CARRY-CONVEXITY D4 and D7,
  WAVE3 WP-3a and WP-13): the direct PID on the price error plus a second PID on
  the fast stabilizers' aggregate /position/ (the BUCK the desk, convertibility
  undertakings, facility and seeder absorbed or issued), so that K works their
  inventory back to zero once they have absorbed a dump (mid-ranging) instead of
  leaving them stranded when their absorption quiets the price error. With the
  position gains at zero it is the Direct controller state for state. It is
  deployed only beside the ops (pro-rata) basket's desk; the canonical machine
  stack and every current world run Direct (now with the leak). Nothing current
  depends on it. *Still the owner's call*: keep it as the research path for when
  stabilizers exist on the machine basket, or delete it with the ops family.
- *The sandboxes stay and grow*; the dashboard should inspect a sandbox's world
  with all its tabs. Below.
- *webevm: publish our own build until upstream releases the clock*, never
  under upstream's name. Prepared (below); the first publish is the owner's, by
  hand.
- *The control lab*: worth keeping for its shock responses, without its first
  no-agent stages and with the households added. Below.

*** The sandboxes' worlds in the dashboard

The dashboard reads run records from a source (=core/js/dashboard/src/sources.js=:
=HttpRunSource= today, a =BrowserWorldSource= planned), so a sandbox's world is
inspectable with every tab once it writes one:

- /A server-hosted savings world (=ServerLink=): done./ =python -m
  alberta_buck.sim.server --runs DIR= (=make nix-venv-sim-savings
  SIM_SAVINGS_RUNS=DIR=) records each session's world under =DIR= as a run
  record named =savings-<sid>-<time>=, written by the same recorder as every
  other run (checked: a session watched five days wrote 6 frames and 252 agent
  lines). =make nix-venv-dashboard SIM_RUNS=DIR= (or =dashboard-lan=) then shows
  it, live while it grows. Off by default: a public server would otherwise keep
  every visitor's world. The record's control address is empty, so the
  dashboard inspects and does not steer it; the savings page steers.
- /A world in the tab (=LocalLink=): S4 and S7./ The JS world writes the same
  frames, agent lines and events (S4); the sandbox bundles the dashboard's pages
  as an /Inspect/ view whose =BrowserWorldSource= reads them from the Worker's
  frame channel (the same port the savings view reads). Same-origin and
  in-memory: no server, the tab's memory as its limit, the record downloadable
  as a run directory for the Python dashboard to open.
- The wallet sandbox's world is a session too; once the JS world records (S4)
  it gets the same Inspect view.

*** webevm, published as alberta-buck-webevm

=webevm= on npm is upstream's (0.9.1); our clock is upstream PR #23 (open,
mergeable, not yet reviewed). =scripts/webevm-fork.mjs= (=make
nix-webevm-fork-pack=) builds a clean checkout of the fork and packs it as
=alberta-buck-webevm@0.9.1-clock.1=: the fork's =dist= and =src= unchanged
(byte-identical to the tarball vendored before), upstream's README and LICENSE
with a notice on top, and a =package.json= naming the fork, the commit and the PR
(=albertaBuckSource=). =core/js= installs it as =webevm= (the dependency's key
names the folder), so no import changes; the webevm world tests pass on it.
=npm publish --dry-run= accepts it. The release workflow now refuses an
alberta-buck-core with a =file:=, =link:= or git dependency. What remains is the
owner's: =npm login=, =make nix-webevm-fork-publish= once, then
="webevm": "npm:alberta-buck-webevm@0.9.1-clock.1"= in =core/js/package.json=
and a relock (=core/js/vendor/README.md=). When upstream releases: depend on
=webevm= itself and deprecate ours.

*** The control lab in the standard system

/What it is./ =alberta_buck/sim/control_lab/= (2,688 lines; =doc/control-lab.toml=,
=doc/control-lab-campaign.toml=; =make nix-venv-control-lab-interactive=): eleven
stages that switch actors on one at a time (01 external prices only, 02 monitor a
replayed trace, 03 K against the replay, 04 linked markets, 05 basket upkeep, 06
K moves, 07 the desk, 08 a price-sensitive buyer, 09 a price-sensitive issuer, 10
both, 11 both and the desk); price profiles (a three-phase sine of 12% over 30
days, mixed, a 30% ten-day step on one TOKEN at day 45, a decade of
commodity-like paths at a month per day), random walks at 0, 0.5 and 1, and buyer
and issuer rates low, medium and high; 891 selector choices from 279 distinct
120-day pyrevm runs at four samples a day, cached by fingerprint, rendered into
an offline page with a note per stage. Its value is the /paired/ comparison: the
same feed, liquidity, seed and starting K in every arm, so the difference between
two stages is the effect of the actor added.

/It no longer runs./ Since 9e0364d3 its arbitrage actor (a =BasketMachineWheel=)
reverts at =setStartMode=, and its books read the legacy signed Buck
(=signedBalanceOf=, =reliefOf=, =mint(uint256)=), which its =equity-ops= world no
longer deploys. Its 14 chain tests are now strict expected failures
(=alberta_buck/test/unported.py=) until C5 removes them. Repairing it in place
would mean redefining every actor's account on the net Buck: the port's work,
done on a world nobody else uses.

/Portable; not too unique./ It runs the same contracts (the basket machine with
its upkeep facet, the Direct controller) on the same pyrevm and day loop. What is
its own maps onto what the standard system has or nearly has:

| lab | standard system |
|-----+-----------------|
| price profiles | =scenario.prices= modes beside =history= and =revert=: =three-phase= and =commodities= (from =control_lab/commodities.py=), with a walk amplification |
| the step shock and other perturbations | =price_shock {token, mult, from_day, until_day}= interventions (they exist); a ramp and a pulse to add |
| the stage ladder | a /cast/ axis of an economy campaign: agent classes on and off, paired by the keyed random streams |
| the abstract buyer and issuer | the households: savers and investors demand BUCKs, debtors supply them; the lifecycle mix sets the balance |
| 279 cached runs and the selector page | an =alberta_buck.sim.economy= campaign (snapshot, parallel cells, report), each cell a run record carrying its axis values; a /Compare/ view in the dashboard |
| poking the world and watching | the dashboard's live session with operator shocks (R2), already |
| four samples a day; the monitor and pair-observer panes (stages 02 to 04) | dropped with those stages, as the owner suggests; daily frames |

/The port, in steps./
- [ ] /C1. Price profiles./ =three-phase= and =commodities= as =scenario.prices=
  modes with =walk=; =ramp= and =pulse= interventions beside =price_shock=.
- [ ] /C2. A frozen-K knob./ The lab's 05 (basket, K fixed) is the baseline
  every later arm is read against: the controller's gains at zero by a
  =deploy= setting, recorded in the manifest.
- [ ] /C3. The =shocks= campaign./ Casts: (a) basket only, K fixed; (b) K
  moves; (c) households (=economy.toml='s population); (d) the lifecycle mix
  (=economy-lifecycle.toml=); the desk only if it joins the canonical stack.
  Profiles: three-phase, step, commodities; walks 0, 0.5, 1; two seeds; 120
  days. 72 cells; an economy day costs about 17 s on pyrevm here (economy-5y),
  less while the population is small, so the grid is a few hours on datasim.
  Each cell a run record whose manifest names its campaign and axis values; the
  per-cast notes (the lab's stage notes, rewritten for the households) in the
  campaign file.
- [ ] /C4. Compare in the dashboard./ Choose a campaign; one selector per axis
  (the lab page's layout); overlay two or three cells on the Overview charts
  (K, BVIB, BUCK/USD, supply, holders) with the shock windows shaded from the
  interventions file; the cast note beside them.
- [ ] /C5. Retire the lab./ When the campaign reproduces its key comparisons
  (K moving against K fixed; households against none) on the economy world:
  remove =control_lab/=, its configs, targets and tests; its rendered page and
  datasets are outputs, not kept.
